# Tractate Soferim

מסכת סופרים

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim)

כתיבת ספר תורה וספרי קדושה אחרים, קריאת חלקים מן התנ"ך בציבור.

Writing Torah scrolls and other holy books, the public reading of biblical texts.

Sections: Chapter-Halakhah

Location: Talmud/Bavli/Minor Tractates

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): Talmud Bavli, Vilna 1883 ed. (Segments: 227, License: Public Domain)

Version (En): The Minor Tractates of the Talmud, trans. A. Cohen, London: Soncino Press, 1965 (Segments: 227)


## Chapter 1

###### Tractate Soferim 1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:1)


אין כותבין ספרים לא על עורות בהמה טמאה ולא על עורות חיה טמאה ולא נתפרין בגידן ולא נכרכין בשערן והלכה למשה מסיני שכותבין על עורות בהמה טהורה ועל עורות חיה טהורה ותופרן [בגידן] וכורכן בשערן. הלכה למשה מסיני שמסרגלין בקנה וכותבין בדיו על העור שהוא סמוך לו מן המקרא (ירמיהו ל״ו:י״ח) ואני כותב על הספר בדיו:


It is not permitted to write [the sacred] books,¹ *tefillin* and *mezuzoth*² on skins of ritually unclean cattle or on skins of ritually unclean wild animals. Nor may they³ be sewn with their sinews, or [the parchment rolls of *tefillin*] be wound with their hair. It is an oral prescription delivered to Moses at Sinai that [all these] shall be written on the skins of ritually clean cattle or ritually clean wild animals, be sewn together with their sinews and [the *tefillin* rolls] wound with their hair. It is also an oral prescription delivered to Moses at Sinai that [the parchments] shall be ruled with a reed⁴ and the writing on the skin be done with ink. [This latter requirement] has support in Scripture, *And I wrote them with ink in the book*.⁵

footnotes:
¹ The scrolls of the Pentateuch used for the lections in public worship.
² These two nouns are added by GRA and M. For Hebrew and other terms, see the Glossary.
³ The parchment sheets of which the scrolls are made up.
⁴ To guide the hand of the scribe, the letters being written under the ruled lines.
⁵ Jer. 36, 18.


###### Tractate Soferim 1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:2)


שאל אחד את רבי יהושע הגרסי מפני מה כותבין על עורות נבלות וטרפות. אמר לו אמשול לך משל למה הדבר דומה לב׳ בני אדם שנתחייבו הריגה למלכות אחד הרגו מלך ואחד הרגו אספקלטור איזה מהן חשוב זה שהרגו מלך. אמר לו א"כ יהא מותר באכילה. אמר לו הרי הוא אומר (דברים י״ד:כ״א) לא תאכל כל נבלה הכתוב אסרה ואני מה אעשה לך (מכאן שכותבים על עורות נבלה ועל עורות טריפה ואין חוששין שמא עורות לבובין הן):


R. Joshua the grits-dealer was asked: ‘Why is it permitted to write [sacred scrolls] on the skins of *nebeloth* and *ṭerefoth?*’ ‘I will give you an analogy,’ R. Joshua replied. ‘This is similar to the case of two men who were condemned to death by the State, one being executed by the king and the other by the executioner. Which of them is superior? He who was executed by the king.’⁶ and *ṭerefoth* are the effect of an act of God. ‘If so,’ he retorted, ‘they should be permitted to be eaten!’ ‘It is stated,’ replied R. Joshua, ‘*Ye shall not eat any* nebelah.⁷. Scripture has forbidden it, so what can I do for you?’ (From here it is inferred that it is permitted to write on skins of *nebelah* and on skins of *ṭerefah* and there is no need to consider the possibility of their having been pierced at the animal’s heart.)⁸

footnotes:
⁶ Nebeloth
⁷ Deut. 14, 21. E.V. of anything that dieth of itself
⁸ This sentence is enclosed in brackets in some eds. It was a heathen practice to remove the heart from a live animal to be offered as a sacrifice; this, in Rabbinic law, rendered the entire animal, including its skin, forbidden as an idolatrous offering (‘A.Z. 29b, Sonc. ed., p. 145).


###### Tractate Soferim 1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:3)


לוקחין עורות מכל מקום לספרים לתפילין ומזוזות ואין חוששין שמא עורות לבובין הן:


Skins⁹ for the [sacred] books, *tefillin* and *mezuzoth* may be bought anywhere, and one need not consider the possibility that they might be skins which were pierced at the animal’s heart.

footnotes:
⁹ Before they were dressed; but dressed skins may be bought only from trust-worthy persons who can testify that the dressing had been done in accordance with the law.


###### Tractate Soferim 1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:4)


כותבין על הקלף במקום בשר ועל הגויל במקום שער ואינו רשאי לשנות. הלכה למשה מסיני שאם שינה בזה ובזה אין קורין בו:


The writing on *ḳelaf* must be on the side of the flesh, and on *gewil* it must be on the side of the hair, no deviation from this being permitted. It is an oral prescription delivered to Moses at Sinai that if one changed one for the other, [the scroll] may not be used for the lection [in the statutory services].


###### Tractate Soferim 1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:5)


אין כותבין לא על דיפתרא ולא על גבי נייר מחוק לא בשחור ולא בשיחור ולא בקומוס ולא בקנקנתום ולא במי טריא ואין נותנין קנקנתום בדיו:


[The sacred texts]¹⁰ may not be written on *difthera* or erased paper;¹¹ nor with coal,¹² shoe-black, ink containing gum, vitriol or fruit-juice.¹³ Vitriol may not be added to the ink.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Mentioned in Rule 1.
¹¹ Others, ‘palimpsest’. GRA omits ‘erased’.
¹² Or, ‘black pigment’.
¹³ Others, ‘rain-water’, ‘wine-lees’.


###### Tractate Soferim 1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:6)


אין כותבין לא עברית ולא ארמית ולא מדית ולא יונית. כתב בכל לשון בכל כתבים לא יקרא בו עד שתהא כתובה אשורית:


[The sacred texts] may not be written in [obsolete] Hebrew characters,¹⁴ or in the Aramaic, Median or Greek languages.¹⁵ and *mezuzoth*. If they were written in any other language or in any other characters they may not be used for the lection [in the statutory services], since they must be written in the Assyrian [script].

footnotes:
¹⁴ The script must be ‘Assyrian’ (the modern Hebrew square characters) and the language ‘the holy tongue’.
¹⁵ According to Meg. 9a (Sonc. ed., p. 48) it is permitted to write the Torah in Greek although this is not allowed in the case of tefillin


###### Tractate Soferim 1:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:7)


מעשה בה׳ זקנים שכתבו לתלמי המלך את התורה יונית והיה היום קשה לישראל כיום שנעשה העגל שלא היתה התורה יכולה להתרגם כל צרכה:


It once happened that five elders wrote the Torah for King Ptolemy¹⁶, I, p. 90).] in Greek, and that day was as ominous for Israel as the day on which the golden calf was made, since the Torah could not be accurately translated.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Presumed to be Ptolemy Philadelphus who reigned over Egypt 285-247 B.C.E. He arranged for the Greek Version, the Septuagint, to be made, as narrated in the next paragraph. [The Tractate gives two accounts of the translation, the present one being the later which reflects the harmful effects which the Septuagint had in arresting the development of exegesis among the Hellenistic Jews (cf. Weiss, Dor


###### Tractate Soferim 1:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:8)


שוב מעשה בתלמי המלך שכנס ע"ב זקנים והושיבם בשבעים ושנים בתים ולא גלה להם על מה כנסם נכנס לכל אחד ואחד מהם אמר להם כתבו לי תורת משה רבכם. נתן המקום עצה בלב כל אחד ואחד והסכימה דעתן לדעת אחת וכתבו לו תורה בפני עצמה: וי"ג דבר שינו בה. ואלו הן אלהים ברא בראשית (בראשית א׳:כ״ו) ויאמר אלהים אעשה אדם בצלם ובדמות (שם) ויכל בששי וישבות בשביעי (שם ב) זכר ונקבה בראו (שם ה) הבה ארדה ואבלה שם (שם יא) ותשחק בקרוביה לאמר (שם יח) כי באפם הרגו איש וברצונם עקרו אבוס (שם מט) ויקח משה את אישתו ואת בניו וירכיבם על נושאי אדם (שמות ד׳:כ׳) ומושב בני ישראל אשר ישבו בארץ מצרים ובארץ כנען שלשים שנה וארבע מאות שנה (שם יב) ואל זאטוטי בני ישראל לא שלח ידו (שם כד) לא חמד אחד מהם נשאתי (במדבר ט״ז:ט״ו) את צעירת הרגלים (ויקרא י״א:ו' דברים י״ד:ז') אשר חלק ה׳ אלהיך אתם להאיר לכל העמים תחת כל השמים (דברים ד׳:י״ט) אשר לא צויתי לעבדם (שם י"ז:ג'):


It also happened that King Ptolemy assembled seventy-two elders and placed them in seventy-two [separate] rooms without telling them the reason for which he had assembled them. He then went to each one of them and said to him,¹⁷ ‘Write for me [a translation of] the Torah of Moses your master’. The Omnipresent inspired them¹⁸ and the mind of all of them was identical, so that each on his own¹⁹ wrote the [same translation of the] Torah, introducing [the same] thirteen alterations²⁰ as follows: ‘God created in the beginning’.²¹ (Gen. 1, 1) the Heb. of which might be misinterpreted to mean that a power named ‘Bereshith’ (in the beginning) created God. ‘And God said I shall make a man in image and likeness.’²² (Gen. 1, 26). The plural *us* and *our* might erroneously suggest a plurality of deities. ‘And He finished on the sixth [day] and rested on the seventh [day].’²³ (Gen. 2, 2) which could be understood to imply that God did some work on the seventh day. ‘Male and female He created him.’²⁴ (*ibid.* V, 2) from which it might be inferred that man and woman were, from the first, two separate beings, contrary to *ibid.* II, 21. ‘Come let Me go down and there confound their language.’²⁵ (*ibid.* XI, 7). ‘And Sarah laughed among her relatives,²⁶ XVIII, 12). The Heb. means ‘within herself’ as Abraham had done when he *laughed … in his heart* (*ibid.* XVII, 17) and had not been rebuked. saying.’ ‘For in their anger they slew oxen²⁷ and in their self-will they digged up a stall.’²⁸ (*ibid.* XLIX, 6) to ‘oxen’ and *oxen* to ‘stall’ in order to remove the stigma of murder from Jacob’s sons. ‘And Moses took his wife and his children and set them upon a carrier²⁹ of men.’³⁰ (Ex. 4, 20). ‘Now the time that the children of Israel dwelt in Egypt and in the land of Canaan and in other lands³¹ was four hundred and thirty years.’³² ‘And he sent the elect of the children of Israel.’³³ (*ibid.* XXIV, 5), the former being regarded as more suitable persons for the service. ‘And upon the elect of the children of Israel He laid not His hand.’³⁴ (*ibid.* 11). The alteration was not essential, but ‘elect’ which was mentioned earlier was preferred. It should be noted that in the total of thirteen alterations, this and the preceding are counted as one. ‘I have not taken one desirable thing³⁵ (Num. 16, 15) to avoid the suggestion that Moses did not take an ass but more precious objects. from them.’ ‘The [beast] with small legs.’³⁶ (Lev. 11, 6) which is *arnebeth*, so as not to give offence to Ptolemy the name of whose queen was Arnebeth. ‘Which the Lord thy God hath allotted to give light unto all the peoples under the whole heaven.’³⁷ ‘Which I commanded should not be served.’³⁸. This might be taken to mean that God did not desire their existence and their creation was consequently due to a power beyond His control. It should be noted that the last two alterations are regarded as one in the enumeration because both deal with heavenly bodies as objects of worship.

footnotes:
¹⁷ lit. ‘to them’.
¹⁸ lit. ‘put counsel in the heart of each one of them’.
¹⁹ So GRA.
²⁰ Deviations from the traditional text. In the extant Versions of the Septuagint only some of these are found.
²¹ Instead of In the beginning God created
²² The Heb. text reads: Let us make man in our image, after our likeness
²³ For the Heb.: And He finished on the seventh day
²⁴ Instead of created them
²⁵ Instead of let us go down
²⁶ i.e. she laughed in the presence of people and therefore incurred censure (ibid.
²⁷ So GRA, H and M. V incorrectly ‘a man’.
²⁸ Changing men
²⁹ So M. V and H read ‘carriers of men’.
³⁰ i.e. an animal conformable with the dignity of Moses, instead of ass
³¹ So M. V omits ‘and in other lands’. [The reading of the Septuagint is ‘in the land of Canaan and in the land of Egypt’.]
³² Instead of the Heb. which omits ‘in the land of Canaan and in other lands’ (Ex. 12, 40), and implies that all the 430 years were spent in Egypt when, in fact, they could not have dwelt there more than 210 years (cf. Rashi to Meg. 9a).
³³ So GRA and Meg. 9a but omitted in V. ‘Elect’ is substituted for young men
³⁴ Here also ‘elect’ was substituted for the Heb. for nobles
³⁵ A ‘desirable thing’ [which occurs in the Septuagint] for the Heb. ass
³⁶ Instead of the Heb. for the hare
³⁷ The insertion ‘to give light’ (Deut. 4, 19) removes the possible misunderstanding that the heavenly bodies enumerated in the verse were intended by God to be objects of worship.
³⁸ Deut. 17, 3 reads which I have commanded not


###### Tractate Soferim 1:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:9)


אין כותבין בזהב. מעשה בתורתו של אלכסנדרוס שהיו כל אזכרותיה כתובות בזהב ובא מעשה לפני חכמים ואמרו תגנז:


[The sacred scrolls] may not be written in gold. It is related that in an Alexandrian³⁹, II, p. 55]. scroll of the Torah all the divine names were written in gold, and when the matter came before the Sages they decided that it should be stored away.⁴⁰

footnotes:
³⁹ So M. V and H read ‘a scroll of the Torah of Alexander’ [i.e. Alexander Jannaeus (103-76 B.C.E.) according to Krauss, Lehnwörter
⁴⁰ lit. ‘it shall be hidden’; not to be used for the lection.


###### Tractate Soferim 1:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:10)


כל התורה כולה עברית היא אלא שיש דברים תרגום של עברית אל יכתוב תרגום ודברים של תרגום אל יהפכם כגון יגר שהדותא גלעד (בראשית לא) ויש אומרים לא דברו אלא על [יגר] שהדותא ועל גלעד שהן שתי לשונות של עברית ותרגום. עברית שכתבו תרגום ותרגום שכתבו עברית אל יקרא בו. ואיזהו תרגום כגון יגר שהדותא גלעד וחביריו. האזינו השמים שעשאה שירה שירה שעשאה האזינו רצוף שעשאה מסורג מסורג שעשאה רצוף או שעשה את המסורג שלא כהלכתו אל יקרא בו:


All the Torah is in Hebrew but it contains some Aramaic words.⁴¹). What is in Hebrew may not be written in Aramaic and the Aramaic words may not be altered; for example, *Jegar-sahadutha* and *Galeed*.⁴² Others hold that the rule applies only to *Jegar-sahadutha* and *Galeed* because they are two specifically Aramaic and Hebrew expressions respectively.⁴³). If Hebrew words were written in Aramaic or Aramaic words in Hebrew, the scroll may not be used for the lection. The Aramaic [and Hebrew] words referred to are *Jegar-sahadutha, Galeed* and the like.
If *ha’azinu hashshamayim*⁴⁴, the opening words of Deut. 32, 1-43, which is arranged in the scroll as follows:
_________________
_________________
_________________ was arranged like the Song of Moses⁴⁵ or if the Song of Moses was arranged like *ha’azinu;* if continuous lines were written in broken ones or vice versa; [35*b*] or if the breaking up was not done according to the rule, the scroll may not be used for the lection.

footnotes:
⁴¹ lit. ‘translation’ (Targum
⁴² Gen. 31, 47, ‘the heap of witness’ in Aramaic and Hebrew.
⁴³ In the case of the other parts, however, although one language should not be changed for another, the scroll is not disqualified if a change was made (cf. N.Y. ad loc.

###### Tractate Soferim 1:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:11)


גמר כל הדף ושייר בה פחות מסרגו ועושה אותו דף קטן בפני עצמו ואינו נמנע:


If one finished [writing nearly] all the Torah and less than a column⁴⁶ remained of the text, one need not hesitate to make of this a small column on its own.⁴⁷

footnotes:
⁴⁶ So GRA and H. V reads: ‘If one finished all the column and left over less, one extends the wording so as to make’, etc. A reading in M is ‘if one finished all the book and left less than a column, one extends the writing so as to make’, etc.
⁴⁷ The extension of a short passage over a full column is done either by enlarging the characters or, according to others, by making each successive line shorter than the one preceding it.


###### Tractate Soferim 1:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:12)


יריעה של ששה חולקין אותה לשנים ושל חמשה אין חולקין אותה שמא יעשה אחד שנים ואחד שלשה. ואינו רשאי לכתוב אלא א"כ יודע לקרות:


A [parchment] sheet [of the Torah scroll] containing six columns may be divided into two sheets;⁴⁸ but one of five columns may not be divided, in order that it should not⁴⁹ be separated into one of two⁵⁰ and one of three columns. No one is allowed to write [a scroll of the Torah] unless one is able to read.⁵¹

footnotes:
⁴⁸ Each containing three columns.
⁴⁹ So GRA and N. Y. H and M read: ‘perhaps one might’.
⁵⁰ Not less than three columns may be contained in each sheet.
⁵¹ M inserts: ‘one is not allowed to write unless [the parchment] was ruled; and if an unruled sheet was written on, it is disqualified’.


###### Tractate Soferim 1:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:13)


ס"ת שכתבו צדוקי או מסור או גר או עבד או שוטה או קטן אל יקרא בו זה הכלל כל הכותבו מוציא את הרבים ידי חובתן:


A scroll of the Torah that was written by a Sadducee,⁵², a Jewish heretic or Jewish-Christian. an informer,⁵³ a proselyte,⁵⁴), one who does not observe the commandments but, on account of domicile in the land of Israel, has renounced idolatry. a slave, a woman,⁵⁵ a madman or a minor may not be used for the lection.⁵⁶ This is the rule: Whosoever cannot act in religious matters on behalf of the public is not permitted to write a scroll of the Torah.⁵⁷

footnotes:
⁵² An adherent of a sect which was opposed to the teachings and traditions of the Pharisees. M adds min
⁵³ Against his fellow-Jews to the ruling Roman authorities. M adds משומד, an apostate.
⁵⁴ This refers to a ‘proselyte settler’ (ger toshab
⁵⁵ So GRA and M; not in V and H.
⁵⁶ For the unsuitability of the persons enumerated, cf. Giṭ. 45b (Sonc. ed., p. 199).
⁵⁷ So GRA. V reads: ‘everyone who writes it [must be a person who] may act on behalf of the public [in discharging a religious obligation]’.


###### Tractate Soferim 1:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/1:14)


פתוחה שעשאה סתומה סתומה שעשאה פתוחה הרי זה יגנז. איזהו פתוחה כל שלא התחיל בראש השיטה. ואיזהו סתומה כל שהניח באמצע השיטה. וכמה יניח בראש השיטה ותהא נקראת פתוחה כדי לכתוב שם של שלש אותיות. וכמה יניח באמצע השיטה ותהא נקראת סתומה כדי לכתוב שם של שלש אותיות. גמר כל הפרשה בסוף הדף ישייר שיטה אחת למעלה ואם שייר מלמטה כדי לכתוב שם של שלש אותיות מתחיל מלמעלה :


If an open section was written as closed or vice versa, the scroll must be stored away. What is an open section? One which does not begin at the beginning of a line. And what is a closed section? [One which is separated from what precedes by a vacant] space in the middle of the line. And how much space must be left at the beginning of a line so that a section may be called ‘open’? A space in which a name of three letters may be written.⁵⁸ And how much space must be left in the middle of a line so that a section may be called ‘closed’? A space in which a name of three letters may be written. If an entire section was completed at the end of a column, one vacant line must be left at the top [of the next column]; but if a vacant space in which a name of three letters may be written was left at the foot [of the column], one begins [the next column] from the top.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ The view of Maimonides which has been adopted is that space must be left for three words, each of three letters (cf. Y.D. 275, §2).



## Chapter 2

###### Tractate Soferim 2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:1)


(מניחין בין שם לשם כדי שיהיו ניכרין ובאותיות כדי שלא יהא מעורבבין אם עירב את האותיות או שהפסיק באמצע השם אל יקרא בו):


Sufficient space must be left between words¹ so that they can be distinguished, and between letters so that they be not mixed. If the letters were mixed or a break was made in the middle of a word, the scroll must not be used for the lection in the statutory services.²

footnotes:
¹ lit. ‘between name and name’.
² In V Rule 1 is enclosed within brackets, probably because it is included in the fuller version in the next Rule.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:2)


מניחין בין שיטה לשיטה כמלא שיטה ובין תיבה לתיבה כמלא אות ובין אות לאות כמלא שער (ובין דף לדף כמלא אגודל). עשה סוף הדף לתחלתו פסול. עירב את האותיות או שהפסיק באמצע השם אל יקרא בו:


Between lines there must be left the space of a line, between words the space of a letter, between letters that of a hairbreadth, and between columns the space of the width of a thumb. If [a word at]³ the end of a column was copied⁴ at the beginning [of the next column, the scroll] is disqualified. If the letters were mixed or if a break was made in the middle of a word, the lection may not be read from it.

footnotes:
³ So according to GRA.
⁴ lit. ‘made’.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:3)


מניחין בין דף לדף שם בן ארבע אותיות. לא יכתוב שנים בסוף הדף ושתים בין דף לדף אבל לא משם קטן ואם היה שם קטן בפני עצמו של שלש אותיות אסור:


It is permitted to insert⁵ between columns [part of] a word of five⁶ letters. One may not write two letters within⁷ the column and three without;⁸ but it is not so with a short word.⁹ If the [part of a longer word, however, forms] a short word of two¹⁰ letters on its own,¹¹ it is forbidden [to insert it between the columns].¹²

footnotes:
⁵ lit. ‘place’.
⁶ So N.Y. V, H and M read ‘four’.
⁷ So N.Y. V and H have ‘at the end’.
⁸ Because the greater part of the word must appear within the column. So N.Y. V, H and M read ‘and two between the columns’. GRA emends to ‘one at the end of the column and three between the lines’.
⁹ Of two letters (N.Y.). One letter may be written between columns (H).
¹⁰ So GRA. V, H and M, ‘three letters’.
¹¹ In a noun like שלחן (table), for instance, which consists of four letters, the last two can form the word חן (grace).
¹² The reading of GRA and N.Y.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:4)


מניחין בין דף לדף בתורה ריוח שתי אצבעות ריוח ובנביאים ובחומשין ריוח גודל אחד. מניחין מלמטה בתורה ריוח טפח ולמעלה שני חלקים בטפח. ובנביאים ובחומשין שלש אצבעות מלמטן ושתים מלמעלן. כאן וכאן אם רצה להוסיף מוסיף ובלבד שלא יהו הרוחות מרובין מן הכתב. מניחין בין ספר לספר בתורה ארבעה שיטין ובנביאים שלש שיטין:


In a Torah scroll the space¹³ of two finger-breadths must be left between columns, but in scrolls of the Prophets and ordinary Pentateuchs¹⁴ a space of one thumb-breadth only. In the lower margin of a Torah scroll the space of a handbreadth is left, and in the upper margin three fingerbreadths;¹⁵ but in scrolls of the Prophets and ordinary Pentateuchs three finger-breadths in the lower margin and two finger-breadths in the upper. In all these cases,¹⁶ if it is desired to extend [the margins] this may be done, provided they do not exceed [the space of] the written matter. Between the books¹⁷ in a Torah scroll a vacant space of four lines must be left, and in a scroll of the Prophets three lines.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹³ So M, H and N.Y. V repeats ‘space’ unnecessarily.
¹⁴ Intended for study only and not for public lections.
¹⁵ So H in agreement with Men. 30a (Sonc. ed., p. 195). V and M read ‘two parts of a handbreadth’.
¹⁶ lit. ‘here and there’.
¹⁷ lit. ‘between a book and a book’. The Torah scroll consists of five books.
¹⁸ Both in the case of the major and minor Prophets (N.Y.).


###### Tractate Soferim 2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:5)


גמר כל הספרים ושייר בו דף אחד עושה אותו יריעה אחת קטנה ואינו נמנע. מניחין [אותו] בסוף הדף כדי להקיפו עושה עמוד בסוף הספר בתחלה כדי להקיפו וא"צ לעשות כן בתחלתו ובתורה מכאן ומכאן לפיכך גוללין הספר לתחלתו ולתורה לאמצעית ואין פותחין את התורה ביריעה ואורכו של ס"ת ששה טפחים:


If one finished [writing nearly] all the book¹⁹ and one column of text remained, one need not hesitate to make a small sheet of it.²⁰ At the conclusion of the [last] column [sufficient parchment] must be left to wind [round the scroll]. It is proper²¹ to attach a roller to the end of a scroll for winding, but there is no need to attach one at the beginning of a scroll. In a Torah scroll, however, rollers should be attached at both ends. A scroll²² is therefore rolled up towards the beginning but a Torah scroll²³ towards its²⁴ centre. A Torah scroll may not be opened by [holding on to] a sheet;²⁵ and the height²⁶ of such a scroll must be six handbreadths.

footnotes:
¹⁹ So N.Y. V, H and M read ‘books’. The reference is either to one book of the Prophets or to several written in one scroll.
²⁰ Cf. I, Rule 11.
²¹ lit. ‘in the beginning’, ‘at the outset’.
²² Of the Prophets, having its cylinder at the end of the scroll.
²³ Which has cylinders at both ends.
²⁴ So H, M and GRA. V omits the pronoun.
²⁵ Owing to its sanctity. It must be opened by manipulating the cylinders. Another reading for פותחין is פוחתין (H, cf. N.Y.) which gives the clause the meaning that when a Torah scroll is used for reading the lection not less than a full sheet, which contains not fewer than three columns, must be kept unrolled throughout the reading.
²⁶ lit. ‘length from top to bottom’.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:6)


אין פוחתין ביריעה פחות משלשה דפין ולא מוסיפין על שמנה. אבל בשיטין נותנין טעם במסעות ארבעים ושנים וברבבות של ישראל ששים ובזקנים של ישראל שבעים ושנים ובתוכחות של משנה תורה תשעים ושמנה הכל לפי הכתב. במסעות שנאמר (במדבר ל״ג:ב׳) ויכתב משה את מוצאיהם. ברבבות ישראל שנאמר (שמות לד) כתב לך [וגו׳] ואת ישראל מה ישראל בששים ריבוא אף שיטה של תורה בששים (ריבוא). בזקנים שבעים ושנים שנא׳ (במדבר י״א:ט״ז) אספה לי שבעים איש וגו׳. וישאירו שני אנשים במחנה [וגו'] והמה בכתובים שבעים ושנים. ובתוכחות תשעים ושמנה שנאמר (דברים כ״ח:נ״ח) אם לא תשמור לעשות את כל דברי התורה הזאת [וגו׳] ובקלפים לא נתנו שיעור אלא כל שרוצה מוסיף ובלבד שלא יפחות משלשה דפין :


A sheet of a Torah scroll must contain not less than three columns and not more than eight. As regards the lines [in a scroll] a reason was given [for four different numbers]: corresponding to the journeys,²⁷ forty-two; corresponding to the myriads of Israel, sixty; corresponding to the elders of Israel, seventy-two; and corresponding to the reproofs in Deuteronomy, [36*a*] ninety-eight; all depending on [the size of] the script.²⁸
‘Corresponding to the journeys,’ as it is said, *And Moses wrote their goings forth*.²⁹) occurs in the same context as the journeys (*goings forth*), the number of lines in a *written* column may be the same as the number of the journeys. ‘Corresponding to the myriads of Israel,’ as it is said, *Write thou these words … and with Israel;*³⁰ and *Israel* (numbering sixty myriads) occur in the same context. as Israel numbered sixty myriads so may the lines of the Torah be sixty [in a column]. ‘Corresponding to the elders, seventy-two,’ as it is said, *Gather unto Me seventy men*³¹ … *there remained … in the camp … of them that were recorded*.³² 26. The number of the elders is found in the same passage as the expression of writing (*recorded*, lit. ‘written’). ‘Corresponding to the reproofs, ninety-eight,’ as it is said, *If thou wilt not observe to do all the words of this law that are written*.³³ refers to the reproofs numbering 98 and occurs with *written* in the same passage.
In the case of *ḳelaf* parchments no limit was prescribed, and one may add [as many lines]³⁴ as desired, provided only there are not less than three columns [to the sheet].³⁵

footnotes:
²⁷ The reading of H and GRA. V and M have ‘in the journeys’.
²⁸ The larger the characters the fewer the number of lines in a column.
²⁹ Num. 33, 2. As the expression of writing (wrote
³⁰ Ex. 34, 27. Write
³¹ Num. 11, 16.
³² ibid.
³³ Deut. 28, 58, the words of this law
³⁴ N.Y. explains, ‘sheets’.
³⁵ i.e. the lines in a column, or according to N.Y. the sheets, should not be so many as to leave less than three columns for the last sheet of the scroll.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:7)


יריעה שבלתה לא יטול שנים ויחזיר שנים אלא נוטל ג׳ ויחזיר ג׳ ומה שהוא מחזיר כמדת כתב ראשון:


If a sheet [of a Torah scroll] is worn out, one may not remove two [columns only]³⁶ and replace them by two new columns; but three columns should be removed and replaced by three new ones, and the new columns must be written in the same size as the original script.

footnotes:
³⁶ So M. N.Y. (cf. Ṭur Y.D. 280) explains, ‘sheets’ instead of ‘columns’.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:8)


שיעור הדף כדי שיהא רואהו ובקטן לא יפחות מטפח. ר׳ יוסי בר יהודה אומר לא יפחות משלש אצבעות:


The width of a column [in a Torah scroll] must be such as to be recognizable at one glance,³⁷ and in a small scroll not less than a handbreadth. R. Jose b. Judah says: It must not be less than the width of three fingers.

footnotes:
³⁷ i.e. not more than three Heb. words, each of ten letters, could be written in a line. If the column is wider, the reader experiences difficulty in passing from line to line.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:9)


ולא יעשה חצי ארכו [יתר] על רחבו ולא רחבו יותר על הצי ארכו אלא *מאמצעו הוא ועושה אותו מן המובחר:


Half the length [of a column]³⁸ must not be greater than its width, nor its width greater than half its length, but they must be exactly alike,³⁹ and this is the most correct procedure.⁴⁰

footnotes:
³⁸ Of text. This is exclusive of the upper and lower margins. Others explain, ‘sheet’, instead of ‘column’.
³⁹ So GRA. The reading of V is: ‘he takes the middle course and so does it in’, etc.
⁴⁰ Some versions omit ‘half’ twice in this paragraph which agrees with B.B. 14a (Sonc. ed., p. 67): ‘A scroll of the Torah should be such that its length does not exceed its circumference nor its circumference its length [when rolled up]’. On this reading, the paragraph deals with the size of the scroll in its entirety.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:10)


לא יעשה חציו גויל וחציו קלפים אבל עושה חציו גויל וחציו צבאים קלפים מפני שאינו מן המובחר:


A Torah scroll may not consist of half *gewil* and half *ḳelaf*, but it may consist of half *gewil* and half *ḳelaf*⁴¹. of deers, although⁴² this is not the best procedure.

footnotes:
⁴¹ So Asheri (cf. N.Y.). V and H have the plural of ḳelaf
⁴² GRA and others. V reads ‘because’.


###### Tractate Soferim 2:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/2:11)


אין דובקין בדבק ואין כותבין על גבי מטלית ואין תופרין במקום הכתב. אמר ר׳ שמעון בן אלעזר משום ר׳ מאיר דובקין בדבק וכותבין על גבי מטלית אבל אין כותבין במקום הכתב מבפנים אלא מבחוץ. צריך שיהא משייר מלמעלן ומלמטן שלא יקרע ומחו לה אמוחא מלמטה למעלה ומלמעלה למטה . הלכה למשה מסיני ספר תורה שנקרע מטלה עליו מטלית מבחוץ. כל האותיות הכפולים באל"ף בי"ת כותב את הראשונים בתחילת התיבה ובאמצע התיבה והאחרונים בסוף ואם שינה פסול. (ספר שנקרע מטלה עליו מבפנים אבל לא מבחוץ):


[A tear in a parchment sheet of a Torah scroll] may not be joined with glue, nor is it permitted to write on a patch, nor may [the sheets] be sewn together on the written side. R. Simeon b. Eleazar said in the name of R. Meir: [A torn sheet] may be joined with glue and it is permitted to write on a patch. It is forbidden, however, to do the sewing⁴³ on the written side within but [it must be done] on the outside.
A space should be left [unstitched] at the top and at the foot [of the sheets] in order that the scroll be not torn [in use].⁴⁴ This, however, was at once qualified by adding: from the foot towards the top and from the top towards the foot. It is an oral prescription delivered to Moses at Sinai that, if a Torah scroll was torn, a patch may be placed on its outside.
In the case of the double letters of the alphabet,⁴⁵ the first ones⁴⁶ are written at the beginning and in the middle of a word, and the second⁴⁷ at the end. If this is reversed,⁴⁸ the scroll is disqualified. If a scroll⁴⁹ is torn, a patch may be placed on the inside and⁵⁰ it is not [obligatory to attach it] on the outside.⁵¹

footnotes:
⁴³ GRA, H and M. According to V ‘the writing’.
⁴⁴ By leaving a part unstitched, the liability to tear when the scroll is rolled up would be reduced. GRA reads this paragraph as follows: ‘It is an oral prescription delivered to Moses at Sinai that a space should be left [unstitched] at the top and at the foot [of the sheets]. This, however, was at once qualified [by the Sages who questioned whether this was such an oral prescription or merely a rule] so that the scroll should not be torn [cf. Meg. 19b, Sonc. ed., pp. 118f]. If a Torah scroll was torn a patch may be placed on its outside’.
⁴⁵ The five letters כ׳, מ׳, נ׳, פ׳, צ׳ which have a different form as finals.
⁴⁶ i.e. the letters in the non-final form.
⁴⁷ The final letters.
⁴⁸ A final letter in the middle or at the beginning or vice versa.
⁴⁹ Other than a Torah scroll.
⁵⁰ So M. V reads ‘but’.
⁵¹ The sentence is omitted in H, his reading being ‘if a scroll was torn’ instead of ‘if a Torah scroll was torn’.



## Chapter 3

###### Tractate Soferim 3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:1)


עושה תורה ונביאים כאחת דברי ר׳ יהודה וחכ"א תורה בפני עצמו ונביאים בפני עצמן:


It is permissible to join the Torah and the Prophets in one scroll, so R. Judah; but the Sages say: The Torah and the Prophets must be written in separate scrolls.¹

footnotes:
¹ So V, H, M. GRA adopts the reading of B.B. 13b (Sonc. ed., p. 66): ‘It is permissible to fasten the Torah, the Prophets and the Hagiographa [in one scroll]’.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:2)


לא יניח בין נביא לנביא כשם שמניח בין ספר לספר בתורה אבל נותן לזה ריוח בפני עצמו [ולזה ריוח בפני עצמו] ומתחיל מלמעלה:


Between a book of the Prophets and another, one should not leave the same empty space as between two books of the Torah, but in each case the space must be that which has been prescribed for it.² Furthermore, [a book of the Prophets]³ must begin at the top.⁴

footnotes:
² viz. four lines between books of the Torah, and three lines between books of the Prophets.
³ If the preceding book ended at the foot of a column.
⁴ Of the next column, the reason being that, if the books of the Prophets were separated one from the other, the beginning of each book would commence at the top of a column. This does not apply to Torah scrolls which may never be cut up into separate books.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:3)


אין עושין תורה חומשין כשהן מפוזרין תורה חומשין מקובצין שאין מורידין מקדושה חמורה לקדושה קלה אבל עושה חומשין תורה אימתי בזמן שמדתן ושיטתן שוין:


A Torah scroll⁵ may not be made into the separate books of the Pentateuch, neither into single books nor into two or three joined together, because it is forbidden to reduce a major sanctity⁶ to a minor sanctity.⁷ From scrolls containing sections of the Pentateuch, however, it is permitted to make up a Torah scroll. When may this be done? When the measurements⁸ and the numbers of the lines⁹ are alike.

footnotes:
⁵ Which contains the five books of Moses.
⁶ The Torah scroll in its entirety.
⁷ A section of the Pentateuch.
⁸ Of all the scrolls that are to be joined together.
⁹ In each column.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:4)


אין עושין שני חומשין כאחת ולא חומש ומחצה כאחת אבל אם עתיד הוא להוסיף עליהן מותר:


It is not permissible to join together two sections of the Pentateuch or one and a half of them to form a single scroll.¹⁰ But if it was intended¹¹ that additional scrolls be attached to them,¹² it is permissible.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Because it might be mistaken for a proper Torah scroll.
¹¹ When these were written.
¹² To complete a Torah scroll.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:5)


(מעשה בבייתוס בן זונן שהיו לו שמנה נביאים מדובקין באחת ע"פ ר"א בן עזריה וי"א לא היו לו כי אם כל אחד ואחד בפני עצמו ושוב מעשה באדם אחד שהביא לפני חכמים תורה נביאים וכתובים כאחת:


It is related that Boethus b. Zonin¹³ had eight books of the Prophets¹⁴ joined together in one scroll in accordance with a ruling of R. Eleazar b. ‘Azariah. Others maintain that he had them all in separate scrolls. It is also related that a certain person brought before the Sages a single scroll containing the Torah, the Prophets and the Hagiographa.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹³ [A pious Jew at the end of the first cent. C.E., not to be identified with the Boethus named in ARN V.]
¹⁴ Joshua, Judges, Samuel, Kings, Isaiah, Jeremiah, Ezekiel and the Twelve Prophets reckoned as one book.
¹⁵ And, as recorded in B.B. 13b (Sonc. ed., p. 66), the Sages ruled that it was a proper scroll.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:6)


בד"א בדיפתרי אבל בגיליון תורה בפני עצמו ונביאים בפני עצמן):


This¹⁶ applies only to a codex,¹⁷ but in the case of a scroll¹⁸ the Torah and the Prophets must each consist of a separate parchment.

footnotes:
¹⁶ That the Torah, Prophets and Hagiographa may be included in one scroll.
¹⁷ Since, as a book for study, no one could attribute to it the sanctity of a Torah scroll.
¹⁸ In that form only the contents could show whether it was a Torah scroll only.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:7)


ספר שפסקו ושנוקד ראשי פסוקים שבה אל יקרא בו גורפו או שעירב בו את האות אל יקרא בו:


If a Torah scroll has spaces or stops [to mark] the beginning of verses, it may not be used for the lections.¹⁹ So also if a letter has been engraved or joined to another, the scroll may not be used for the lections.

footnotes:
¹⁹ Because the Torah scroll must conform to the original form without any alterations.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:8)


ספר שנמחק אל יכתוב בו ר"ש בן אלעזר אומר אם היה בבואה שלו נכרת הרי זה מותר:


A scroll [some of whose letters] are faded may not be used for the lections.²⁰ R. Simeon b. Eleazar says: If the outline [of the effaced letters] is distinguishable, it is permitted [to rewrite them].

footnotes:
²⁰ So H and GRA. V reads ‘one should not rewrite them’.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:9)


ספר שנמחק בו שיטה אחת על פני כולו אסור נמחק בו רובו ומיעוטו קיים מותר. ספר שיש בו טעות אל יקרא בו וכמה יהא אחד בדף דברי רבי יהודה רשב"ג אומר אפילו שלשה בדף אל יקרא בו:


A scroll of the Torah in which a whole line is faded may not be used for the lections. If the greater part of a line is faded and the smaller part intact, the use of the scroll is permitted. If a Torah scroll contains an error, it may not be used for the lections. How many [errors disqualify it]? One in a column, is the view of R. Judah. R. Simeon b. Gamaliel says: Even if there be one error in three columns²¹ the scroll may not be used for the lections.

footnotes:
²¹ The reading of GRA. In V and M, ‘even three errors in a column’. The reading of H is: ‘Three in a column is the view of R. Judah. Rabban Simeon b. Gamaliel says: Even if there be one error in one column’.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:10)


אין פוחתין בתורה פחות מג׳ דפין אבל קורין באחד ואין נמנעין ומצוה בגדול לגללו הגולל ס"ת גוללו כנגד התפר אבל לא כנגד היריעה ס"ת לא יהדקנו מתוכו אבל מהדקו מבחוץ והנותן ס"ת לחבירו לא יתנהו אלא בימין והמקבל לא יקבלנו אלא בימין שכן נתינתו מהר סיני שנאמר (דברים ל) מימינו אש דת למו א"ר שפטאי א"ר יוחנן הקורא בלא נעימה והשונה בלא זמרה עליו הכתוב אומר (יחזקאל כ׳:כ״ה) וגם אני נתתי להם חוקים לא טובים:


Not less than three columns of the Torah scroll [may be unrolled at one time during the reading], but there is no objection to the reading of ten [columns] in one [lection]. It is a religious precept that the most important member²² shall roll up the scroll.²³ When one rolls up a Torah scroll he should roll it [so that it closes] at the seam²⁴ and not at [the middle of] a sheet.²⁵ A Torah scroll should [36*b*] be rolled up²⁶ from without,²⁷ but the [band]²⁸ should be tied from within.²⁹
When one hands a Torah scroll to another person it must be done with the right hand only, and he who receives it must also do so with the right hand only, because the Torah was delivered at Mount Sinai in this manner, as it is stated, *At His right hand was a fiery law unto them*.³⁰
R. Shefaṭai³¹ said in the name of R. Joḥanan: If one reads the Scripture without a melody³² or learns the Mishnah without a tune,³³ of him Scripture says, *Wherefore I gave them also statutes that were not good*.³⁴ which one learns. If such are described as *not good* it is because the melody is lacking.

footnotes:
²² Of the congregation, or of those who have taken part in the reading.
²³ Thus showing honour to the Torah.
²⁴ So that if the scroll is pulled, only the seam will be affected.
²⁵ Which might be torn by the pull.
²⁶ So GRA. V and M: ‘it shall not be fastened from within, but it must be fastened from without’.
²⁷ i.e. the back of the written side, while the scroll is being held up by another person who is faced by the written side.
²⁸ Which is wound around the scroll to prevent it from unrolling.
²⁹ The knot facing the written side. In this way, when the next lection is to be read, the knot can be easily undone without having to turn the scroll over.
³⁰ Deut. 33, 2.
³¹ In Meg. 32a (Sonc. ed., p. 194) the name is Shefaṭiah.
³² Indicated in the printed Pentateuch by the traditional cantillation.
³³ Which aids the memory.
³⁴ Ezek. 20, 25. An accompanying melody enlivens one’s studies, and endears the statutes


###### Tractate Soferim 3:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:11)


לא יניח כותבין דיו בקולמוס על גבי הכתב ולא יאחזו בכפליים ויתירם ולא יאחזו ויוציא הכתב מבחוץ ולא יוציאנו מתוכו ויחלצנו וכולן במגיהי ספרים מותר לא ישמט אדם ס"ת מחיקו ויחלצנו (בו) שנוהג בו דרך בזיון ולא יתננו ע"ג ארכבותיו ויתן אצילו עליו ויהא קורא כדברי ר׳ נחמיה:


A scribe may not put upon the written part [of a Torah scroll] a reed-pen with ink on it; nor may he take hold of the sheets of parchment³⁵ and thereby unroll [the scroll]; nor may he grasp them to extract the written sheets;³⁶ nor may he remove a scroll from its [case] by shaking it out. All these [restrictions] are relaxed with the official revisers of Scripture scrolls. A Torah scroll may not be released from its case while it is lying on one’s lap³⁷ because this is disrespectful treatment. It is not permissible to place a Torah scroll on one’s knees or lean on it with one’s elbows while reading; so according to the ruling of R. Nehemiah.

footnotes:
³⁵ So H. V, ‘folds’.
³⁶ Which are rolled up; lit. ‘and bring forth the writing without’.
³⁷ lit. ‘one shall not slip a Torah scroll from one’s lap and release it’.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:12)


לא יתננו על גבי הכסא ויהא תלוי וקורא כשם שקורין בשטרות שאין נוהגין בזיון בספרים אלא אוחז בידיו באימה וקורא בו אין זורקין ספרים ממקום למקום ואין נוהגין בהן דרך בזיון:


A Torah scroll may not be placed over a chair [with its sheets] hanging down while it is being read, in the manner of ordinary documents, because [sacred] books must not be treated with disrespect. The proper procedure is³⁸ to hold it in the hands³⁹ with reverence and so read it. [Sacred] books⁴⁰ may not be thrown about⁴¹ nor may they be treated in [any other] disrespectful manner.

footnotes:
³⁸ lit. ‘but’.
³⁹ By means of a cloth since, according to Rule 16 below, it is forbidden to touch a Torah scroll with the bare hands.
⁴⁰ Not only Torah scrolls (Maimonides).
⁴¹ lit. ‘from place to place’.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:13)


לא יתננה על גבי מטה (לא) במרגלות המטה ולא תחת המטה ולא ישב אדם על גבי המטה שס"ת עליה מעשה ברבי אלעזר שישב לו על גבי המטה וספר עליה ועמד דומה כמי שנשכו נחש שנאמר (ויקרא י״ט:ל׳) את שבתותי תשמורו ומקדשי תיראו לא מן השבתות אני ירא אלא ממי שפקד עליו [ולא] מן המקדש אני ירא אלא ממי שפקד עליו (שנא׳ את שבתותי תשמורו ומקדשי תיראו). חייב אדם לעשות ציצית נאים ומזוזות נאות ולכתוב ס"ת נאה בדיו נאה וקולמוס נאה ולבלרין נאים ובקלפים נאים ועורות צבועים ויעטפנו בשיראין נאין שנאמר (שמות ט״ו:ב׳) זה אלי ואנוהו עשה לפניו מצות נאות כדברי ר׳ ישמעאל. ר"ע אומר הרבות בנאות שלו אבא שאול אומר הדמה לו מה הוא רחום וחנון אך אתה רחום וחנון:


A scroll of the Torah may not be placed on a bed and [certainly]⁴² not at its foot or under it; nor may one sit on a bed on which a [Torah] scroll [happens to] rest. It is related that R. Eleazar once sat [unwittingly] on a bed on which lay a [Torah] scroll and [when he realized it] he jumped up as if a snake had bitten him. [Respect is shown for the Torah] because Scripture states, *Ye shall keep My sabbaths, and reverence My sanctuary*⁴³—it is not the sabbaths that one reverences but Him who commanded [to keep them], nor is one to reverence the sanctuary but Him who commanded [to make] it.
It is obligatory to make beautiful *ẓiẓith*, beautiful *mezuzoth*, to write a beautiful scroll of the Torah with choice ink, with a fine reed-pen [written] by an expert penman⁴⁴, I, p. 254n לבלר אומן for לבלרין נאים (fine pen-men).] on well-finished parchments, on deer skins,⁴⁵ and to wrap it in precious silks; for Scripture states, *This is my God, and I will glorify Him*,⁴⁶ (ואנוהו) is connected homiletically with נאה, ‘beautiful’, and understood as ‘I will serve Him in a beautiful manner’. which means, perform the commandments in a beautiful manner to His glory.⁴⁷ Such is the interpretation of R. Ishmael. R. ‘Aḳiba expounds the text as meaning that one should expatiate on His excellencies. Abba Saul explains: Be like Him;⁴⁸ as He is merciful and gracious so be thou merciful and gracious.

footnotes:
⁴² Cf. N.Y. The addition of ‘certainly’ avoids an anticlimax.
⁴³ Lev. 19, 30.
⁴⁴ [Reading with Bacher, Ag. d. Tannaiten
⁴⁵ So GRA. V and H, ‘dyed skins’.
⁴⁶ Ex. 15, 2, I will glorify Him
⁴⁷ lit. ‘before Him’.
⁴⁸ He explains ואנוהו as the equivalent of אני והוא, ‘I and He’.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:14)


אין נוהגין בזיון באוכלין ואין זורקין אוכלין ממקום למקום לא ישב אדם על גבי קופה מלאה תמרים או גרוגרות אבל יושב הוא על גבי קופה מלאה קיטניות או ע"ג עגול של דבילה מפני שנהגו כן. אין סומכין ואין מכסין באוכלין (ואין אוכלין אוכלין באוכלין אלא אם כן ראויין לאכילה ר׳ אליעזר בן יעקב אומר כל שיש לו יד ועוקץ מותר לכסות בו ר' שמעון בן גמליאל אומר כל אוכל שגומרו בידי אחרים מותר לכסות ר׳ שמעון בן גמליאל אומר כל אוכל שיש לו שומר מותר לכסות והכותב בספרים לא יהפוך מגילה עליה שנוהגין בהן דרך בזיון:


Foodstuffs may not be treated with disrespect, nor may they be thrown about. One may not sit on a basket containing dates or dried figs;⁴⁹ but he may sit on a basket which contains pulse or on a cake of pressed figs, because it is customary to do so.⁵⁰ Foodstuffs may not be used as a support⁵¹ or as a covering, nor may foodstuffs be eaten with the aid of foodstuffs,⁵² unless [the latter] are fit to be eaten.⁵³ R. Eliezer b. Jacob says: Any fruit which has a stem or stalk⁵⁴ may be used as a covering.⁵⁵ R. Simeon b. Gamaliel says: Any foodstuff which requires processing may be used as a covering.⁵⁶ R. Simeon b. Gamaliel also says: Any foodstuff which has a protection⁵⁷ may be used as a covering. One who writes [Torah] scrolls may not turn over a sheet on its face⁵⁸ because this is disrespectful treatment.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ Because they might be crushed and spoilt.
⁵⁰ Pulse or dried figs are not spoilt by sitting on them.
⁵¹ For any object.
⁵² Using them, e.g., in place of a spoon.
⁵³ Immediately after use and would not be thrown away.
⁵⁴ Whereby it can be lifted if it should happen to fall into the contents of the vessel which it covered.
⁵⁵ Since it would not be necessary to throw it away after use.
⁵⁶ In its raw state, since its use causes no deterioration.
⁵⁷ e.g. a shell.
⁵⁸ lit. ‘scroll’, with the writing downwards to protect it from falling dust while it is drying.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:15)


אם סיים כל הספר ושייר בה דף א׳ עושה אותו יריעה קטנה ואינו נמנע אין מכסין באוכלין) ר"מ אומר כל שיש לו קליפה מותר לכסות בו ר' יהודה אומר כל שיש לו עוקץ או יד מותר לכסות בו:


If one finished [writing nearly] all the [Torah] scroll and left over one column, there is no objection to his arranging it on a small sheet [on its own].⁵⁹
Foodstuffs may not be used as a covering.⁶⁰ R. Meir says: Any fruit which has a husk may be used as a covering. R. Judah says: Any fruit which has a stem or a stalk may be used as a covering.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Cf. I Rule 11 and II Rule 5.
⁶⁰ This and the following are repeated from Rule 14.


###### Tractate Soferim 3:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/3:16)


אין אוכלין אוכלין באוכלין אלא אם כן היה אוכלן בבת אחת וספר שאין עליו מפה הופכו על הכתב אמר רבי יוחנן כל האוחז ספר תורה ערום נקבר ערום ערום ס"ד אלא נקבר ערום בלא אותה מצוה:


Foodstuffs may not be eaten with the aid of foodstuffs unless they are consumed simultaneously. If a scroll cannot be covered with a cloth,⁶¹ it may be turned over on its writing.⁶² R. Joḥanan said: Whoever holds a Torah scroll without a covering⁶³ will be buried without a covering.⁶⁴ Buried without a covering! Is this thinkable? It means, Without the covering protection of that religious performance.⁶⁵

footnotes:
⁶¹ lit. ‘on which there is no’. To protect the wet ink from dust while it is drying.
⁶² This qualifies what was stated in Rule 14 in accordance with ‘Erub. 98a.
⁶³ lit. ‘naked’; this is regarded as disrespectful.
⁶⁴ i.e. a shroud.
⁶⁵ (65). The reverential treatment of the Torah scroll is accounted an act of piety.



## Chapter 4

###### Tractate Soferim 4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:1)


אלו שמות שאין נמחקין הכותב אל"ף למ"ד אל"ף דל"ת יו"ד ה"י שדי צבאות אהיה אשר אהיה רבי יוסי אומר צבאות חול אמר ר"ש בן אלעזר של בית אגודה היו כותבין בירושלים והיו מוחקין צבאות שהיו נוהגין בו חול שנאמר (דברים כ׳:ט׳) ופקדו שרי צבאות בראש העם:


The following divine names may not be erased,¹ viz. *Alef-lamed*,² (God). *Alef-daleth*,³ (Lord). *Yod-he*,⁴. *Shaddai*,⁵ *Ẓeba’oth*,⁶ *’Eheyeh ’asher ’Eheyeh*.⁷ R. Jose says: *Ẓeba’oth* is a secular noun.⁸ R. Simeon b. Eleazar said: The Aguddah⁹ family, who were scribes in Jerusalem, used to erase *Ẓeba’oth*, because they treated it as a secular noun, as it is stated, *Captains of* hosts *shall be appointed at the head of the people*.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹ lit. ‘he who writes’.
² Pronounced ’El
³ The first two letters of the divine name Alef-daleth-nun-yod
⁴ The first half of the Tetragrammaton and also constituting a name of God, Yah
⁵ Almighty.
⁶ [Lord of] hosts.
⁷ ‘I am that I am’ (cf. Ex. 3, 14).
⁸ And may be erased.
⁹ [H reads חגירה in agreement with j. Meg. 71d.]
¹⁰ Deut. 20, 9. Here צבאית refers to the army of Israel.


###### Tractate Soferim 4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:2)


הכותב אל"ף למ"ד מארבע אותיות יו"ד ה"י מד' אותיות אינן נמחקין מפני שיש שמות כיוצא בהן שי"ן דל"ת משדי צבא מצבאות א׳ ה׳ מאהי' הרי אלו נמחקין מפני שאין שמות כיוצא בהן:


If one writes *Alef-lamed* of the four letters [of *Alef-lamed-waw-he*]¹¹ or *Yod-he* of the four letters [of *Yod-he-waw-he*]¹² they may not be erased, because there are divine names corresponding to them.¹³ *Shin-daleth* of *Shin-daleth-yod* (*Shaddai*) or *Ẓadi-beth-alef* of *Ẓadi-beth-alef-waw-taw* (*Ẓeba’oth*) or *Alef-he* of *Alef-he-yod-he* (*’Eheyeh*) may be erased, because there are no divine names corresponding to them.

footnotes:
¹¹ Meaning ‘God of’.
¹² The Tetragrammaton.
¹³ אל and יה.


###### Tractate Soferim 4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:3)


כל האותיות המשמשות לשם בין מלפניו בין מלאחריו הרי אלו נמחקין בה׳ כה׳ לה׳ וה׳ בכל"ו הרי אלו נמחקין וכיוצא בהן ומלאחריו אלהינו אלהיכם אלהיהם נ"ו כ"ם ה"ם הרי אלו נמחקין וכיוצא בהן ויש אומרים כ"ם מאלהיכם אינו נמחק:


All prefixes and suffixes connected with a divine name may be erased. [Prefixes such as] *in* the Lord, *like* the Lord, *to* the Lord, *and* the Lord [represented respectively by the letters] *beth, kaf, lamed, waw* and suffixes such as *our* God, *your* God, *their* God [represented respectively by the letters] *nun-waw, kaf-mem, he-mem* and others of the same kind, may be erased. Some Sages say: The *kaf-mem* of ‘your [God]’¹⁴ must not be erased.

footnotes:
¹⁴ And so also all other suffixes (cf. Y.D. 276, §9), because the divine name which precedes the suffix imparts sanctity to it (N.Y.).


###### Tractate Soferim 4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:4)


וכל שמות הדיוטות שאותיותיהם אותיות השם הרי אלו נמחקין כי יודע אלהים כי ביום אכלכם ממנו ונפקחו עיניכם והייתם כאלהים (בראשית ג׳:ה׳) הראשון קודש והשני חול כי ה׳ אלהיכם הוא אלהי האלהים (דברים י׳:י״ז) הראשון קודש והשני חול:


All ordinary nouns the letters of which are the same as those of a divine name may be erased.¹⁵ [For instance in] *For God doth know that in the day ye eat thereof, your eyes shall be opened, and ye shall be as God*,¹⁶ the first [divine name] is sacred and the second¹⁷ according to the Targum it means ‘as angels.’ is secular; in *For the Lord your God, He is God of gods*,¹⁸ the first¹⁹. is sacred and the second²⁰, signifying judges or princes. is secular.

footnotes:
¹⁵ Since they possess no sanctity
¹⁶ Gen. 3, 5.
¹⁷ as God;
¹⁸ Deut. 10, 17.
¹⁹ i.e. the first two, your God, He is God
²⁰ of gods


###### Tractate Soferim 4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:5)


ואדני האדנים (שם) הראשון קודש והשני חול אלהי אברהם (בראשית ל״א:נ״ג) קודש ואלהי נחור (שם) חול אלהי אביהם (שם) חול אלהים לא תקלל (שמות כ״ב:כ״ז) משמע קודש וחול ר"ש אומר קודש (שנאמר) (בראשית ל״ג:י׳) כי על כן ראיתי פניך כראות פני אלהים הרי זה חול מחנה אלהים (שם לב) הרי זה קודש:


In *Lord of lords*,²¹ the first is sacred and the second is secular; in *the God of Abraham*²² it is sacred; in *the God of Nahor*²³ Nahor did not worship the true God. it is secular; in *the God of their father*²⁴ Terah (their father) was an idolater. it is secular. In *Thou shalt not revile God*²⁵ [the noun may bear] a sacred or secular meaning.²⁶ R. Simeon maintains that the noun is sacred.²⁷ In *Forasmuch as I have seen thy face, as one seeth the face of God*²⁸ the noun is secular;²⁹ in *God’s camp*³⁰ XXXII, 3. the noun is sacred.

footnotes:
²¹ Deut. 10, 17.
²² Gen. 31, 53.
²³ ibid.;
²⁴ ibid.;
²⁵ Ex. 22, 27.
²⁶ It may denote God or judges.
²⁷ V inserts ‘as it says’ within brackets, the words being redundant. [H omits and reads ‘Ishmael’ instead of ‘Simeon’.]
²⁸ Gen. 33, 10.
²⁹ Because it refers to an angel or prince.
³⁰ ibid.


###### Tractate Soferim 4:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:6)


כל השמות האמורים באברהם קודש חוץ מאחד שהוא חול שנא׳ (שם יח) ויאמר אדני אם נא מצאתי חן בעיניך וי"א [קודש] (שם כ) ויהי כאשר התעו אותי אלהים ר׳ חנינא אחיו של ר׳ יהושע אומר קודש שאלולי אלהים כבר התעו אותי:


All the names [signifying God] mentioned in connection with Abraham are sacred, except one which is secular, viz. where it is stated, *And he said: My Lord, if now I have found favour in thy sight*.¹ XVIII, 3. *My lord* was addressed to the leader of the three angels. Others say that it is sacred.³¹ was addressed to God. In *And it came to pass, when God caused me to wander*³² XX, 13. the name is secular.³³ R. Ḥanina the brother³⁴, I, p. 385.] of R. Joshua maintains that it is sacred [since the verse means] that, had it not been for God,³⁵ they³⁶ would long ago have *caused me to wander*.³⁷ (the wicked men) *caused me to wander, God of my father’s house was with me* (to help me). [For various interpretations of the verse, cf. Midrash Rabbah, Genesis, LII, §11 (Sonc. ed., I, p. 457).]

footnotes:
¹ (30a) ibid.
³¹ My Lord
³² ibid.
³³ So GRA. By ‘God’ is meant princes or rulers who were the cause of Abraham’s wanderings.
³⁴ [He reads ‘nephew’ in agreement with Shebu. 33b (Sonc. ed., p. 205). The correct form of the name is Ḥananiah, Bacher, Ag. d. Tannaiten
³⁵ Who protected him.
³⁶ The wicked rulers.
³⁷ The verse will accordingly bear the following interpretation: And it came to pass, when they


###### Tractate Soferim 4:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:7)


כל השמות האמורים בלוט חול חוץ מהאחרון (שם יט) ויאמר לוט אליהם אל נא אדני. כל השמות האמורים במיכה חול ר׳ יוסי אומר ביו"ד ה"י קודש באל"ף למ"ד חול חוץ מבית האלהים (אשר) בשילה (שופטים י״ח:ל״א) וכל השמות האמורים בנבות קודש חוץ מברך נבות אלהים ומלך (מלכים כא) וכל השמות האמורים בגבעת בנימין ר׳ אליעזר אומר חול ר׳ יהושע אומר קודש אמר לו רבי אליעזר וכי היאך (יכול) אפשר שהמקום מבטיח ואינו עושה אמר לו ר׳ יהושע המקום (כבר) מבטיח ועושה :


All [divine] names mentioned in connection with Lot are secular³⁸ except the last, viz. *And Lot said unto them: Oh, not so my Lord*.³⁹, and then his petition to God begins, *My Lord, behold*, etc. [So the Targum and Shebu. 35b (Sonc. ed., pp. 205f).] All [divine] names mentioned in the story of Micah⁴⁰ are secular. R. Jose says: When they begin with *Yod-he*⁴¹ they are sacred, but when with *Alef-lamed*⁴² they are secular except in the phrase *the house of God was in Shiloh*.⁴³ XVIII, 31. All the [divine] names which occur in the story of Naboth are sacred except in the sentence *Naboth did curse god*⁴⁴. *and king*.⁴⁵ All the [divine] names which occur in the narrative of Gibeah of Benjamin⁴⁶ are secular according to R. Eliezer, but R. Joshua says: They are sacred. R. Eliezer said to him, ‘Is it possible that the Omnipresent would promise [victory]⁴⁷ and not fulfil?’⁴⁸ 21, 25). R. Joshua replied, ‘The Omnipresent promises and fulfils’.⁴⁹ XX, 28). [37*a*]

³⁸ There is only one secular name in the Lot passages. The Tetragrammaton, which is of course sacred, occurs twice. The word ‘all’ is not intended to be taken literally (cf. N.Y.).
³⁹ Gen. 19, 18. Lot’s address to the angels is taken to end at not so
⁴⁰ Judg. 17f.

⁴³ ibid.

###### Tractate Soferim 4:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:8)


אספרה אל חוק (תהילים ב׳:ז׳) הרי זה (קודש וחכ"א הרי [זה]) חול אל אלהים בציון (שם פד) הראשון חול והשני קודש להלוך אל אל במשפט (איוב ל״ד:כ״ג) הראשון חול והשני קודש עמנואל (ישעיה ז) ועמנו אל (שם ח) הראשון (נחלק) [חול] והשני קודש הראשון [אינו] נחלק והשני (אינו) נחלק:


In *I will tell* [*’El*] *of the decree*⁵⁰ the word *’El*⁵¹ is sacred;⁵² but the Sages maintain that it is secular.⁵³. In *’el God in Ẓion*⁵⁴ the first is secular⁵⁵ meaning *before* (E.V.). and the second is sacred. In *When he should go ’el God in judgment*⁵⁶ the first is secular and the second [*’Elohim*] is sacred. In the case of *Immanuel*⁵⁷ and *Immanu’el*⁵⁸ VIII, 8. the first is secular⁵⁹ and the second⁶⁰ is sacred. The first must not be divided⁶¹ but the second is divided.⁶²

footnotes:
⁵⁰ Ps. 2, 7.
⁵¹ Heb. אל with a difference of vowels may mean ‘God’ or ‘to’.
⁵² The sense of the text being ‘I will tell God of the decree’.
⁵³ The text means, as in E.V., I will tell of the decree
⁵⁴ Ps. 84, 8.
⁵⁵ ’El
⁵⁶ Job 34, 23.
⁵⁷ Isa. 7, 14.
⁵⁸ ibid.
⁵⁹ Being the name of a child.
⁶⁰ Meaning ‘God is with us’.
⁶¹ Into two words, since it forms a single name.
⁶² Because it consists of two separate words, עמנו (with us) אל (God). The reading adopted here is that of H and N.Y.


###### Tractate Soferim 4:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/4:9)


ויהי כאשר התעו אותי אלהים (בראשית כ׳:י״ג) ומי אל כמוך (מיכה ז) הראשון חול והאחרון קודש תאשם שומרון כי מרתה באלהיה (הושע י״ד:א׳) הרי זה קודש רבי נתן אומר בבית אלהיו (שם ט) הרי זה קודש ואתה באלהיך תשוב (שם יב) הרי זה קודש אלהים נצב בעדת אל בקרב אלהים ישפט (תהלים פב) משמש קודש וחול יסגירני אל אל עויל (איוב ט״ז:י״א) הראשון קודש והשני חול כי אל אל (העמוד) [האמר] נשאתי (שם לד) הראשון חול והשני קודש ואמר ר"א בנו של ר"י הגלילי ואל יהי מקום (לשועתי) [לזעקתי] (שם טז) יש לאל ידי (בראשית ל״א:כ״ט) ואין לאל ידך (דברים כ״ח:ל״ב-ל״ג) בהיות לאל ידך (משלי ג׳:כ״ז) אל (מותי) [מות] (שם ב) כולם חול ואלהים אמר לבהלני (דברי הימים ב ל״ה:כ״א) קודש חדל לך מאלהים אשר (עמדי) [עמי] (שם) קודש כדברי ר' יוסי בר יהודה אלוהים זדים קמו עלי (תהלים פו) קודש אלא שהקורא צריך שיהא מפסיק בקריאתו אלהים. זדים קמו עלי. אבל רחום וחנון ארך אפים ורב חסד מלך מלכים מרומם גדול עליון צדיק וישר חסיד תמים גבור הרי אלו נמחקין והמקלל בהן עצמו וחבירו חייב גוים ומתים אחד חייב דיין ונשיא חייב שתים וי"א על הנשיא חייב שלש המקלל (בהן) אביו [ואמו] בשם המיוחד הרי זה טעון סקילה ועל הכינויים הרי הוא באזהרה:


[In the verses] *And it came to pass when they*⁶³. *caused me to wander, God*, etc.,⁶⁴ and *Which god*⁶⁵. *is like unto Thee*⁶⁶ the first⁶⁷ in Micah 7, 18. are sacred and the second⁶⁸ in Gen. and *Thee* in Micah. are secular. [The divine name in] *Samaria shall bear her guilt, for she hath rebelled against her God*⁶⁹ is sacred. R. Nathan says: [In the phrase] *in the house of his God*⁷⁰ IX, 8. it is sacred. In *therefore turn thou to thy God*⁷¹ XII, 7. it is sacred. In *God standeth in the congregation of God; in the midst of the judges*⁷² *He judgeth*⁷³ it is used as sacred⁷⁴. and secular.⁷⁵ In *God delivereth me to*⁷⁶ *the ungodly*⁷⁷ the first is sacred and the second⁷⁸ in the sense of ‘to’. is secular. In *For hath any said unto God: I have borne*⁷⁹ XXXIV, 31. the first [*’el*] is secular and the second sacred.
R. Eleazar the son of R. Jose the Galilean said:⁸⁰ [In the verses] *And let my cry have no*⁸¹, the same consonants as for the name of God. *resting-place;*⁸² *It is in the power*⁸³ (*’el*) equals the divine name. *of my hand; And there shall be nought in the power of thy hand;*⁸⁴ *When it is in the power of thy hand;*⁸⁵ *Unto death*,⁸⁶ II, 18; *unto* is *’el* in Heb. all [the words whose consonants are the same as those for God] are secular. In *God hath given command to speed me*⁸⁷ it is sacred. In *Forbear thee from meddling with God, who is with me*⁸⁸ it is sacred according to R. Jose b. Judah. In *O God, the proud are risen up against me*⁸⁹ it is sacred but the reader must pause in reading [after *God*]:⁹⁰ might erroneously be taken as qualifying God. *God, the proud are risen up against me*.
[The terms] merciful and gracious, long-suffering and abounding in lovingkindness, king, kings, exalted, great, Most High, righteous and upright, pious, perfect, mighty, may be erased.⁹¹ He who curses himself or his neighbour by [any of] these⁹² incurs guilt. [If he curses] heathens or the dead no guilt⁹³ is incurred. [If he curses] a judge or a prince he incurs twofold guilt;⁹⁴ according to others he incurs threefold guilt for cursing a prince.⁹⁵ If a person curses his father or mother with the Tetragrammaton⁹⁶ he is liable to the penalty of stoning,⁹⁷ but if only with the attributes he is liable to a warning.⁹⁸
