## Chapter 10

###### Tractate Soferim 10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:1)


משה [הקנא והתרה כדתנן המקנא לאשתו ר׳ אליעזר אומר מקנא על פי שנים ומשקה ע"פ אחד שאין התראה פחות משנים שאינה יכולה לומר לא התריתי אבל באחד תעיז ותאמר לא התריתי] הקניא להם לישראל שיהו קורין בתורה בשבתות וימים טובים ובראשי חדשים ובחולו של מועד שנאמר (ויקרא כ״ג:מ״ד) וידבר משה את מועדי ה׳ אל בני ישראל:


Moses¹’, an unusual verb to be employed in the sense of ‘warn’. It reads as follows: *Hiḳni*’, i.e. ‘he warned’, as we have learned in a Mishnah (Soṭ. I, 1), *hammeḳannë’* (of the same root as *hiḳni’* which means), If [a husband] ‘warns’ his wife [not to associate with a certain man], R. Eliezer said: His warning is valid if it was given in the presence of two witnesses, though he makes her drink [the water of bitterness] on the evidence of one witness [who saw that she had secluded herself with the man after she had been given due warning], because a warning is not valid if given in the presence of less than two witnesses; for [only where there are two witnesses] would she be able to plead, ‘I was not warned’, but when [there was only] one witness she might dare to plead, ‘I was not warned’. warned² some eds. read the normal verb *hithḳin*, ‘he established a custom, ordained’. Israel that they should read out of the Torah³ on Sabbaths, Festivals, New Moons and the intermediate days of the Festivals;⁴ as it is stated, *And Moses declared unto the children of Israel the appointed seasons of the Lord*.⁵

footnotes:
¹ In V and H a passage has been interpolated after ‘Moses’ which has no relation to the subject-matter. It seems to have been originally a marginal note explanatory of the meaning of hiḳni
² For hiḳni’
³ In the service of the Synagogue.
⁴ lit. ‘the secular [part] of the Festival’, the days intervening between the holy days which begin and end the Festivals of Passover and Tabernacles.
⁵ Lev. 23, 44.


###### Tractate Soferim 10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:2)


עזרא תיקן להם לישראל שיהו קורין בתורה בשני ובחמישי ובשבת במנחה:


Ezra ordained for Israel that they should read in the Torah on Mondays and Thursdays and in the afternoon service on the Sabbath.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ [For this and other ordinances of Ezra, cf. B.Ḳ. 82a (Sonc. ed., p. 466) where the reason is given for these additional lections.]


###### Tractate Soferim 10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:3)


( אין בין י"ט לשבת אלא אוכל נפש בלבד אין בין שבת ליום הכפורים אלא שזה זדונו בידי אדם וזה זדונו בהכרת):


There is no difference between a Festival and the Sabbath⁷ save only in the matter of preparing food.⁸ There is no difference between the Sabbath and the Day of Atonement save only that a wilful violation of the former [is punishable] by men⁹ while a wilful violation of the latter [is punished] by *kareth*.

footnotes:
⁷ In regard to forbidden work.
⁸ lit. ‘food of a person’. On a Festival, but not when it falls on the Sabbath, the preparation of food for the day by cooking is permitted.
⁹ i.e. a court of law.


###### Tractate Soferim 10:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:4)


א"ר יהודה מקום שמפסיקין שבת שחרית שם קורין במנחה בשני ובחמישי ובשבת הבאה:


R. Judah said: At the place [in the Torah] where we leave off on the Sabbath morning we begin to read at the afternoon service, and on the following Monday, Thursday and Sabbath.


###### Tractate Soferim 10:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:5)


בשני ובחמישי ובשבת במנחה קורין ג׳ אין פוחתין מהן ואין מוסיפין עליהם ואין מפטירין בנביא הפותח והחותם בתורה מברך לפניה ולאחריה בר"ח ובחולו של מועד קורין ארבעה אין פוחתין מהם ואין מוסיפין עליהם זה הכלל כל שיש בו מוסף ואין בו יום טוב קורין ארבעה:


On Mondays and Thursdays¹⁰ and at the Sabbath afternoon service three men read [from the Torah].¹¹ or section is divided for the Sabbath lection. On the specified occasions it is subdivided between three worshippers. Their number must be neither reduced nor increased; nor is a *hafṭarah* read from [a book of] a Prophet. He who begins as well as he who concludes [the reading of the prescribed section of] the Torah recites the appropriate benedictions before and after his reading.¹²
On the New Moon and during the intermediate days of a Festival, four [persons read from the Torah]. Their number must be neither reduced nor increased. This is the general rule: On any day which has a *musaf* and is not a Festival, four persons read [from the Torah].

footnotes:
¹⁰ During the morning service.
¹¹ The first of the seven portions into which the weekly sidra’
¹² For the benedictions, cf. P.B., p. 68.


###### Tractate Soferim 10:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:6)


בי"ט ה׳ ביום הכפורים ששה וביום שבת שבעה אין פוחתין מהם אבל מוסיפין עליהם י"ט שאיסור לאו חמשה יוה"כ שאיסור כרת ששה שבת שאיסור סקילה שבעה ומפטיר בנביא הפותח והחותם בתורה מברך לפניה ולאחריה:


On a Festival five [persons read from the prescribed portion of the Torah]; on the Day of Atonement, six; and on the Sabbath, seven. The number must not be reduced but may be increased. On a Festival, for the violation of which only a negative precept is infringed, five persons [read in the Torah]; on the Day of Atonement, for the violation of which *kareth* is incurred, six; on the Sabbath, the violation of which is punishable by stoning, seven; and one concludes with [a reading from a book of] a Prophet.
The one who begins as well as the one who concludes [the lection] recites the benediction before and after his reading.


###### Tractate Soferim 10:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/10:7)


אין פורסין על שמע לא בעמידה ולא בישיבה ואין עוברין לפני התיבה ואין נושאין את כפיהן ואין מפטירין בנביא ואין עושין מעמד ומושב על אנשים כל שכן לנשים שאין עושין מעמד ומושב לנשים ואין אומרים קדיש וברכו פחות מעשרה רבותינו שבמערב אומרים בשבעה ונותנין טעם לדבריהם בפרוע (שופטים ה׳:ב׳) פרעות בישראל בהתנדב עם ברכו ה׳ כמנין התיבות וי"א אפי׳ ששה ברכו ששה הוא ובמקום שיש שם תשעה או עשרה ששמעו בין ברכו בין קדיש ולאחר התפלה עומד אחד מאלו ויאמר ברכו או קדיש וענו אחריו יצא וכבר תקנו חכמים לחזנים לומר לאחר גאולה (תהילים קי״ג:ב׳) יהי שם ה׳ מבורך מעתה ועד עולם ואחריו ברכו את ה׳ המבורך כדי לצאת אותם שלא שמעו דאמר רבי יוחנן הלואי שיתפלל אדם כל היום כולו ונהגו אנשי מערב ואנשי מזרח לאמרו לאחר עושה שלום בשלש תפלות של שמנה עשרה גזרה משום הנכנסין וגזירה משום היוצאין ואפי' לאחר קריאת ספר תורה. ולענין פדיית קרקעות תשעה וכהן ואדם כיוצא בהן:


The accompanying benedictions of the *Shema‘*¹³ and *yoẓer ’or* (P.B., pp. 37ff) which precede the *Shema‘*, and the three benedictions of the *‘Amidah* which follow it (P.B., pp. 44ff) including the *ḳedushshah*. may not be repeated¹⁴. The ruling applies to persons who had read the morning prayers in private and now wish to join in congregational worship. They do not repeat all the statutory morning prayers, but may repeat ‘broken off’ parts, viz. those specified in the preceding note (cf. N.Y. and Rashi to Meg. 23b). For other interpretations, cf. Meg. (Sonc. ed., p. 141, n. 1) [and J. Rabbinowitz, *Mishnah Megillah*, pp. 114ff]. in congregational prayer, irrespective of whether they are said standing¹⁵ said while the worshippers are standing. or sitting;¹⁶ said while the worshippers are sitting. nor may one act as reader in public worship;¹⁷ nor may [the *kohanim*] lift their hands;¹⁸ nor may the *hafṭarah* be read from the Prophet;¹⁹ nor may halts²⁰ be made [at funerals]²¹ for men, how much less for women since no halts are made for women; nor may *ḳaddish*²² and *bareku* be said²³—if less than ten adult males²⁴ are present.
Our teachers in the West²⁵ rule that seven [adult males²⁶ are sufficient]²⁷ and, as a reason for their ruling, they quote, *When men let grow their hair in Israel, when the people offer themselves willingly, bless ye the Lord*,²⁸ [which implies that the number present must be the same] as that of the [Hebrew] words.²⁹ (*bless ye*). Others hold that even six [adult males are sufficient for the purpose, since up to] *bareku* [the number of words] is six.
Furthermore, where there are nine or ten persons who have heard either *bareku* or *ḳaddish* and, after the service, one of them [who missed one of the benedictions] said *bareku* or *ḳaddish*, he has thereby performed [the duty of public worship].³⁰
The Sages long ago issued an ordinance to Synagogue precentors to say after the prayer for redemption,³¹ *Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth and for ever*,³² and after that, ‘Bless ye the Lord Who is to be blessed’,³³, etc. (P.B., p. 37). so as to enable those who had not heard the benedictions³⁴ to perform the duty of public worship; for R. Joḥanan said: Would that a man read his prayers all the day long.³⁵
The congregations of the West and the congregations of the East³⁶ have a custom to say it³⁷ after ‘He Who maketh peace’³⁸. in the three statutory prayers³⁹ which immediately follows it. The originators of the custom forbid such an interruption. of the Eighteen Benedictions,⁴⁰ as a provision for the benefit of those who arrived late and for those who leave early.⁴¹ [The verse from Psalms with the benedictions is also said] even after the public reading from the Torah.⁴²
As regards the redemption of land,⁴³ however, [the presence is required of] nine male adults and a *kohen*; and the same law applies to the redemption of a human being.⁴⁴

footnotes:
¹³ These are the ḳaddish, bareku
¹⁴ lit. ‘they must not break off in connection with the Shema‘ ’
¹⁵ i.e. the benedictions which follow the Shema‘
¹⁶ The benedictions which precede the Shema‘
¹⁷ In connection with any part of the prayers. Lit. ‘they do not pass before the [reading] chest’, to lead the congregation in the conduct of the service.
¹⁸ To bless the people during the service; cf. P.B., p. 238a.
¹⁹ As is the law in regard to the Torah itself; cf. Meg. 23b (Sonc. ed., p. 141).
²⁰ lit. ‘a standing and a sitting’.
²¹ It was customary, when returning from a burial, to halt and sit seven times to praise and lament the dead.
²² P.B., p. 37.
²³ Even without the other statutory prayers.
²⁴ Who have attained their thirteenth birthday.
²⁵ The land of Israel which was west of Babylon.
²⁶ Who have not heard one of the benedictions indicated above.
²⁷ For the purpose of repeating the benedictions.
²⁸ Judg. 5, 2.
²⁹ Because the verse contains the word bareku
³⁰ Contrary to the ruling of the Rabbis of the West and the others who hold that seven or even six are enough.
³¹ ‘O Rock of Israel,’ etc. (P.B., p. 44).
³² Ps. 113, 2.
³³ Bareku
³⁴ Owing to their late arrival.
³⁵ Therefore it is permissible for the precentor to repeat the benedictions under discussion more than once for the benefit of those who did not hear him when he first read them.
³⁶ The land of Israel and Babylon respectively.
³⁷ The verse from Psalms followed by the benedictions mentioned.
³⁸ P.B., p. 54, forming the conclusion of the ‘Amidah
³⁹ For the evening, morning and afternoon. This custom is contrary to the previous opinion which permits an interruption between the prayer for redemption and the ‘Amidah
⁴⁰ The actual number is nineteen, but it was eighteen when originally instituted.
⁴¹ It is for their benefit that the repetition is not made at the end of the entire service.
⁴² If persons came in as late as that.
⁴³ Which had been dedicated to the Temple.
⁴⁴ Cf. Lev. 27, 2ff, 16ff.



## Chapter 11

###### Tractate Soferim 11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:1)


הקורא בתורה לא יפחות מג׳ פסוקים ולא יקרא למתורגמן יותר מפסוק אחד ובנביא ג' ואם שלשתן של שלש פרשיות קורא אחד אחד כגון (ישעיהו נ״ב:ג׳) [כי] כה אמר ה׳ חנם נמכרתם (וכגון) [וגו׳ כי] כה אמר ה׳ [אלהים] מצרים ירד עמי בראשונה (וכגון) [וגו׳] ועתה מה לי פה נאם ה׳. עיר שאין בה קורא אלא אחד קורא הוא הקריות ובלבד שישב בין קריאה לקריאה:


One who reads the Torah [in public worship] should read not less than three verses,¹ and he should not read for the interpreter² more than one verse [at a time].³ In the case of [the lection from] a Prophet, however, three [verses at a time may be read];⁴ but if the three verses constituted three different paragraphs, he may read one verse at a time⁵—for instance, *For thus saith the Lord; Ye were sold for nought*⁶ … [39*b*] *For thus saith the Lord God: My people went down aforetime into Egypt*⁷ 4. *… Now, therefore, what do I here, saith the Lord*.⁸ 5.
In a city where only one person is able to read [from the Torah scroll] he reads all⁹ the prescribed sections, provided he sits down between the reading of one section and the next.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹ The number corresponds to the three main divisions of the Bible: the Pentateuch, Prophets and Hagiographa; Meg. 24a (Sonc. ed., p. 143).
² Who translated and expounded the Heb. text into Aramaic for the congregation.
³ So that the interpreter, who had no book before him, should not become confused.
⁴ If desired. Since no instruction in law is involved in the lection from the Prophets as in the Torah, a mistake would not be serious.
⁵ For the interpreter.
⁶ Isa. 52, 3.
⁷ ibid.
⁸ ibid.
⁹ ‘All’ is added by GRA, M and H.
¹⁰ lit. ‘between reading and reading’. The number of times he sits down corresponds to the number of persons who are to be called up for the reading. This ruling applies to the time when only the first and last who were called up recited the benedictions before and after the reading. At the present time, when each person called up recites the benedictions before and after his portion, there is no need for the sitting down.


###### Tractate Soferim 11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:2)


מדלגין בנביא ואין מדלגין בתורה לפי שגוללין נביא ברבים ואין גוללין ספר תורה ברבים וכן אמר ר׳ ירמיה בשם ר"ש בן לקיש אין גוללין ספר תורה ברבים ר׳ יוסי פתר לה כגון שהיתה הפרשה קטנה כדי שישמעו ישראל את התורה על הסדר והא תנינן קורא באחרי מות ואך בעשור שניי' היא שהוא סדרו של (עולם) [יום] ותדע לך דר' שמעון בן לקיש אומר בכל מקום אינו קורא על פה וכאן הוא קורא על פה:


It is permitted to skip from place to place in [a scroll of] a Prophet,¹¹ but not in a Torah scroll, because [the scroll of] a Prophet may be unrolled in public but not of the Torah.¹² Similarly ruled R. Jeremiah in the name of R. Simeon b. Laḳish: A Torah scroll may not be unrolled in public.
R. Jose enquired: Suppose¹³ there was only a small section [which one wished to skip]!¹⁴ The real reason¹⁵ is that Israel shall hear the Torah in its proper order. But have we not learned: [The High Priest] reads [in public] *aḥarë moth*¹⁶, designating the section Lev. 16. and *’ak be‘asor?*¹⁷, designating the section beginning Lev. 23, 27. These sections are far removed from one another and the Torah scroll must consequently be unrolled in public. Why is this permitted? [With these two sections] it is different,¹⁸ because they deal with the statutory order [of the reading] of the day.¹⁹ You can see the reason²⁰ for yourself, because R. Simeon b. Laḳish ruled, ‘No part of the Torah may be²¹ read by heart’, and yet here²² (Num. 29, 7). [the High Priest] reads by heart.²³

footnotes:
¹¹ Omitting the intermediate portions.
¹² The unrolling of which might disturb the congregation.
¹³ GRA in agreement with j.Meg. IV, 5, 75b. V and H read ‘interpreted it, as for instance’.
¹⁴ And consequently no unrolling is required. Why, then, should skipping in such a case be forbidden?
¹⁵ lit. ‘but’ added by GRA. V and H omit.
¹⁶ lit. after the death
¹⁷ lit. howbeit on the tenth
¹⁸ Therefore unrolling in public is permitted.
¹⁹ i.e. the Day of Atonement. As they form one subject, skipping from place to place is allowed.
²⁰ lit. ‘and you may know’.
²¹ lit. ‘in every place one may not’.
²² The section beginning And on the tenth day
²³ For the reason stated above.


###### Tractate Soferim 11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:3)


רבי יוסי הוה מפקיד לרב עולא חזנא דכנישתא דבבלא כד (היה קורא) [היא חדא] אורייתא תהא גיילאה אחורי פרוכתא וכד אינון תרתי תהא מוביל חדא ומייתי חדא עד היכא א"ר יוסי כגון פרשת יהוידע הכהן:


R. Jose gave the following instruction to Bar²⁴ ‘Ula, the precentor of the Babylonian Synagogue [in Sepphoris]:²⁵ When only one Torah scroll is available the unrolling²⁶ should be done in front of the ark curtain,²⁷ but when two scrolls are available you should carry the first [back to the ark] and then take out the other.²⁸ To what extent [is skipping in the Torah permitted]? R. Jose said: As, for instance, the sections²⁹ [read on the Day of Atonement] by the High Priest.³⁰ The reading of V is ‘Jehoiada the priest’.

footnotes:
²⁴ So j.Meg. IV, 5, 75b, the text of which is followed here. V has ‘Mar’.
²⁵ In Upper Galilee.
²⁶ When skipping is necessary from one passage to another.
²⁷ There is no need to return the scroll to the ark after the first reading had concluded and then take it out again for the second section.
²⁸ The present custom is to take both scrolls from the ark at the same time and to keep the second rolled and wrapped until the completion of the reading from the first.
²⁹ Which are not so far from one another as to cause the interpreter to stop and keep the congregation waiting while the scroll is being unrolled to the second section, and deal with the same subject, thus causing no confusion to the hearers.
³⁰ So GRA and H in agreement with j.Meg. loc. cit.


###### Tractate Soferim 11:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:4)


החזן שהוא קורא בתורה בשבת פחות משבע קריות ושכח כסבור שקרא ז׳ יחזור ויקרא וימלא שבע קריות ויפטיר (שמיני) ובלבד שיהא בסדר עשרים ואחד פסוקים ליתן לכל קורא וקורא ג׳ ואם פחות יוסיף על הסדר הזה מפרשה אחרת :


If the precentor read in the Torah on a Sabbath for less than seven persons,³¹ having forgotten and thought that he had already read for³² seven persons, he should read again completing the number of seven, and call up an eighth as *mafṭir;* provided always that the lection³³ consists of [a minimum of] twenty-one verses and allows three verses for each of the seven persons who are called up. And if a section consists of less [than three verses for each of the seven persons]³⁴ an addition should be made to the section from the one following it.³⁵

footnotes:
³¹ lit. ‘readings or callings’, i.e. the lection was allocated to less than the minimum number.
³² Or, ‘had called up’.
³³ lit. ‘the order’, the prescribed section for the reading of the day.
³⁴ This apparently refers to ancient Synagogues where only short portions of the Torah were read or where it was completed once in three years, not annually as is the present custom. Then the section for a Sabbath may sometimes have been very short.
³⁵ lit. ‘from another portion’.


###### Tractate Soferim 11:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:5)


ואם היתה הפרשה של ארבעה חמשה פסוקים קורא את כולה אבל פרשה של ששה פסוקים קורא ומפסיק לפי שיש בה שיעור שתי קריות ואם קרא בשני ובחמישי ובשבת במנחה ובמנחה של י"ט פחות מי' פסוקים יחזור ויקרא עד שישלים עשרה פסוקים ובמה קורין במנחה של י"ט בסדר שפסק לפני הרגל:


If a section consisted of four or five verses, he reads the whole of it, but with a section of six verses he may read and make a break³⁶ because it has the [minimum] length for two readings. If on a Monday or Thursday, or the Sabbath afternoon service or the afternoon service of Festivals³⁷ less than ten verses³⁸ CXXXVII, §1 and commentaries *ad loc.*). Others maintain that in general ten is the minimum number. were read, he should repeat the reading to complete ten verses. From where [in the Torah] does one read in the afternoon service of a Festival [when it occurs on a Sabbath]?³⁹ In the regular order where [the Torah reading] was interrupted before the Festival.⁴⁰

footnotes:
³⁶ At the end of the third verse.
³⁷ On which occasion three persons are called to the reading of the Torah.
³⁸ According to some authorities, if nine verses were read no repetition of the reading is necessary (cf. ’Oraḥ Ḥayyim
³⁹ On a Festival which occurs on a week-day there is no reading from the Torah in the afternoon service.
⁴⁰ i.e. from the beginning of the lection of the next Sabbath.


###### Tractate Soferim 11:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/11:6)


ואם קרא ודילג על פסוק ולא קראו אם קרא עשרה מן הפסוק המדולג ביניהם אינו חוזר ואם בתוך עשרה הוא יחזור ויקרא עשר כראוי על שטתן שאין מדלג זו בחול ובמנחה בשבת ובמנחה של יום טוב אבל בשבת אם שכח ועבר על פסוק ואח"כ נודע לו חוזר וקורא אפי' לאחר שהפטיר בנביא והתפלל מוסף מפסיק מיד וקורא שאין מעמידין תפילת המוספין פעם שנייה:


If in the course of public reading he skipped a verse and omitted to read it, should he have read ten verses exclusive⁴¹ of the verse skipped, he need not go back; but if less than⁴² ten, he must go back and read ten verses in correct order so that none is left out. This is the case with [the reading of] a week-day,⁴³ Sabbath afternoon and Festival afternoon;⁴⁴ but on a Sabbath [morning] if he forgot and passed over a verse and then realized [his mistake], he must stop⁴⁵ and re-read [the passage in full]. Even after one had read the *hafṭarah* in [a Book of] a Prophet and recited the *musaf*, he must stop forthwith and read [the Scriptural passage]. The *musaf*, however,⁴⁶ need not be repeated.

footnotes:
⁴¹ So GRA and H. V has ‘from’.
⁴² lit. ‘within’.
⁴³ i.e. Monday and Thursday.
⁴⁴ Since the omitted verses will be read on the following Sabbath morning in the course of the lection.
⁴⁵ Added by GRA.
⁴⁶ So GRA. V inserts ‘because’ which cannot be justified.



## Chapter 12

###### Tractate Soferim 12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:1)


מפסיקין בברכות ואין מפסיקין בקללות א"ר חייא בר גמדא מה טעם דכתיב (משלי ג׳:י״א) ואל תקוץ בתוכחתו אל תעשם קוצים קוצים:


It is permitted to make a break¹ in the blessings² but not in the curses.³ 14-46. R. Ḥiyya b. Gamda said: What is the reason?⁴ Because it is written [*My son, despise not the chastening of the Lord*,]⁵ *neither cut thou up His correction*⁶.—do not cut them⁷ up into pieces.⁸

footnotes:
¹ i.e. the passage may be divided and two persons called up to the reading.
² e.g. Lev. 26, 3-13.
³ ibid.
⁴ Why may not more than one person be called to the section of the curses?
⁵ This is the first part of the verse which is not quoted in the text of the Tractate.
⁶ Prov. 3, 11, E.V. neither spurn
⁷ lit. ‘make it’, a section in the Torah which deals with God’s chastening of Israel.
⁸ The Heb. root may bear both meanings: ‘spurn’ and ‘cut up’.


###### Tractate Soferim 12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:2)


א"ר לוי אמר הקב"ה אינו דין שיהיו בני מתקללין ואני מתברך אמר ר׳ יוסי בר אבין לא מטעם הזה אלא זה שהוא עומד לקרות בתורה פותח בדבר טוב וחותם בדבר טוב:


R. Levi said:⁹ The Holy One, blessed be He, said: It is not proper that My children shall be cursed while I am being blessed.¹⁰ R. Jose b. Abin said: It is not for this reason, but because one who stands up to read in the Torah must begin and conclude with something auspicious.¹¹

footnotes:
⁹ Explaining the reason that no more than one person may be called to the reading of the curses in Lev. 26, 14ff.
¹⁰ By the recital of the benedictions in the middle of reading the curses.
¹¹ If two persons were to be called to the reading, the first would finish and the second begin with a verse which threatens a curse.


###### Tractate Soferim 12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:3)


לוי בר פאטי שאל לר׳ חנינא אילין ארוריה מאי יקרינון חד ויברך לפניהן ולאחריהן אמר ליה אין לך טעון ברכה לפניו ולאחריו אלא קללות שבתורת כהנים ושבמשנה תורה:


Levi b. Paṭi enquired of R. Hanina, ‘What is the law in regard to the curses each of which begins with *’arur?*¹². Must they be read by one person only,¹³ and must a benediction be recited before and after them?’ He replied, ‘There is no [passage] that requires a benediction before and after its reading but the curses of Leviticus¹⁴, lit. ‘the law of the priests’, the name of the Book of Leviticus. The passage is XXVI, 14-46. and Deuteronomy’.¹⁵, lit. ‘the repetition of the law’, the name of the Book of Deuteronomy. The passage is XXVIII, 15-68. Only with these passages, which are not restricted to a particular time, are the benedictions recited before and after they have been read; but with the *’arur* curses, which were spoken by the Levites only (cf. Deut. 27, 14) and at the time when the Jordan was crossed (*ibid.* 2), no benedictions are to be recited.

footnotes:
¹² Deut. 27, 15-26, each verse commencing Cursed be
¹³ As with the curses of Lev. 26, 14ff which must not be read by more than one person.
¹⁴ Torath Kohanim
¹⁵ Mishneh Torah


###### Tractate Soferim 12:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:4)


ר׳ יונתן ספרא דגופתרא נחית להכא חמא לרבי אבונא ספרא קרא שירת הבאר ובירך לפניה ולאחריה א"ל ועבדין כן א"ל עד כדון את לזו צריך כל השירות טעונין ברכה לפניהם ולאחריהם:


When Jonathan, a children’s teacher from Gufta,¹⁶ came down here,¹⁷ he saw a children’s teacher, R. Abuna, reading the Song of the Well¹⁸ and reciting the benedictions before and after it. ‘Is it proper,’ he asked him, ‘to act in this manner?’¹⁹ ‘Do you,’ the other retorted, ‘still require [a ruling] on this subject? All poems²⁰ must be preceded and followed by benedictions.’

footnotes:
¹⁶ In Galilee. V incorrectly ‘Gufthera’.
¹⁷ Tiberias where R. Abuna re-sided. The city is a on lower level, hence the verb ‘came down’.
¹⁸ Num. 21, 17-20.
¹⁹ The rule is that the benedictions are recited only at the beginning and end of the entire section to be read. The Song of the Well, appearing in the middle of a section, should consequently require no benedictions before and after its reading.
²⁰ Such as the Song of the Sea (Ex. 15, 1-19), the Song of the Well and probably also Deut. 32, 1-43.


###### Tractate Soferim 12:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:5)


אשאלת לרבי סימון אמר לון בשם ר׳ יהושע בן לוי אין לך טעון ברכה לפניו ולאחריו אלא שירת הים ועשרת הדברות וקללות שבמשנה תורה:


A question²¹ was asked of²² R. Simon. ‘There is no section’,²³ he told them in the name of R. Joshua b. Levi, ‘which requires a benediction before and after its reading other than the Song at the Sea, the Ten Words,²⁴ and the curses in Deuteronomy’.²⁵

footnotes:
²¹ In connection with the preceding Rule.
²² Another reading is ‘he came and asked’.
²³ lit. ‘you have not’.
²⁴ The Decalogue (Ex. 20, 2-17).
²⁵ Deut. 28, 15-68. Others add ‘the curses in Leviticus’.


###### Tractate Soferim 12:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:6)


א"ר אבי אני לא שמעתי אלא נראין הדברים בעשרת הדברות רבי יוסי בר אבין בשם רבי בתומנתא פסוקייא אחריה ובמשנה תורה טעונין ברכה לפניהם ולאחריהם:


R. Abahu²⁶ said: I have not heard [such a ruling],²⁷ but it is feasible to apply it to the Ten Words. R. Jose b. Abin said in the name of Rabbi:²⁸, III, p. 727, n. 7.] The last ten verses in Deuteronomy²⁹ require a benediction before and after they have been read.³⁰

footnotes:
²⁶ So GRA and H. V reads ‘Abi’.
²⁷ That in the case of the passages specified benedictions are to be recited before and after the reading.
²⁸ [The reading should be ‘R. Berekiah’ or ‘R. Simon’; cf. Bacher, Ag. d. pal. Amoräer
²⁹ So GRA and N.Y. The text in V is difficult.
³⁰ These verses, which describe the death of Moses, form a section of their own, because they were written by Joshua (according to R. Judah or R. Nehemiah) or, if written by Moses, they were dictated to him by God while he ‘wrote them down with tears’ (B.B. 15a, Sonc. ed., p. 72).


###### Tractate Soferim 12:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/12:7)


ר׳ יצחק סחורה שאל את ר׳ יצחק נפחא ר"ח טבת שחל להיות בשבת במה קורין לענין כלות והמפטיר בשל שבח ור"ח. (שחל) [חל] להיות בחול קורין ביום ראשון שלשה בשל ר"ח והרביעי בשל חנוכה שכל התדיר מחבירו קודם לחבירו והואיל וקרא תחילה (בכל) [בשל] ר"ח ביום שני יקרא ג׳ לחנוכה והרביעי של ר"ח ובראשי חדשיכם עד שישלים (כל השיטה) ולמה כן שלא בא הרביעי אלא מחמת מוסף ראש חדש (ואם היה בשבת תורגמן או דרוש מפטירין בנביא או שלשה פסוקים או חמשה או שבעה ואינו חושש לעשרים ואחד פסוקים):


R. Isaac Seḥorah asked R. Isaac Nappaḥa, ‘What do we read [first]³¹ when the New Moon of *Ṭebeth*³². falls on a Sabbath?’³³ in the second and third. [He replied,] ‘In the section of *kalloth*,³⁴ (Num. 7, 1). This designates the entire section, descriptive of the dedication offerings brought by the princes of Israel, which is prescribed for the reading of the day. while the *mafṭir* reads³⁵ the passages relating to the Sabbath and the New Moon.³⁶. If it falls on a week-day we call up on the first day³⁷ three persons for the reading of the New Moon, the fourth reading that of *Ḥanukkah*, because that which is more frequent takes precedence.³⁸ once a year. But since the reading of the New Moon was read first,³⁹ [40*a*] three persons are to be called up on the second day for the reading of the *Ḥanukkah* passage,⁴⁰. while the fourth reads⁴¹ the section of the New Moon, viz. *uberashë ḥodshekem*⁴² (Num. 28, 11). to the end of the passage.⁴³ 15. Why is this so?⁴⁴ passage? Because it was only on account of the New Moon *musaf* that a fourth person is added’.⁴⁵ there is no *musaf*. This argument cannot be applied to the first day of the New Moon because it is the second day which begins the month. The first of the two days of the New Moon is the last day of the preceding month. V and H insert here a ruling which has no connection with this chapter and is practically identical with XIV, 2 below.

³⁸ The New Moon occurs monthly, Ḥanukkah
³⁹ On the first day of the New Moon.

⁴² E.V. and in your new moons
⁴³ ibid.
⁴⁴ i.e. why is the fourth person called to the reading of the New Moon passage and not, as on the first day, to the Ḥanukkah

אמר ר׳ יוסי בר אבין שירת הלוים לא יפחתו לו מששה קרויות וסימניך הזי"ו ל"ך ואלו הן האזינו. זכור. ירכיבהו. וישמן. לו. כי ידין ה' עמו. ויש לה פסוק שיטה מלאה מלמעלה ושיטה מלאה מלמטה כנגד כך נהגו לעשות שיטותיה של תורה :


R. Jose b. Abin said: For the Song of *Ha’azinu*⁴⁶, designating the section Deut. 32, 1-43. For *Ha’azinu* (the reading of GRA) V and H read ‘the Levites’, because the poem was sung by the Levites in the Temple during the Sabbath *musaf* in six parts on successive Sabbaths. not less than six persons⁴⁷ are to be called up, and your mnemonic sign⁴⁸ is *h z y w l k*,⁴⁹ which stands for *ha’azinu*,⁵⁰ (Deut. 32, 1). *zekor*,⁵¹ (*ibid.* 7). *yarkibehu*,⁵² (*ibid.* 13). *wayyishman*,⁵³ (*ibid.* 15). *lu*,⁵⁴ (*ibid.* 29). *ki yadin … ‘ammo*.⁵⁵ (*ibid.* 36). It must also be provided with⁵⁶ the space of a full line above it and of a full line below it. Corresponding to it the custom has been adopted to write in the Torah its number of lines.⁵⁷

## Chapter 13

###### Tractate Soferim 13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:1)


אבל בשירת דוד שבשמואל ובתהלים לא נתנו חכמים שיעור אבל לבלר מובהק מרצפן במפתחות באותיות וסוף וכן תהלים כולו ואיוב ומשלי:


In regard, however,¹ to the Song of David in Samuel² and in the Psalms,³ the Sages have not prescribed a limit;⁴ but a skilled scribe spaces them out symmetrically⁵ according to the beginnings, the middle pauses, and the endings of the verses.⁶ A similar arrangement is also followed throughout the Psalms and in Job and Proverbs.

footnotes:
¹ In contrast to the poems dealt with in the previous Rule.
² 2 Sam. 22.
³ Ps. 18.
⁴ For the number of lines in a column.
⁵ In the form of stichoi.
⁶ This rendering follows the text in M and H.


###### Tractate Soferim 13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:2)


מגלת אסתר צריכה שירטוט כאמתה של תורה ספר נקרא:


The scroll of Esther⁷. requires ruled lines⁸ as does the real⁹ Torah,¹⁰ because it is called ‘a scroll’.¹¹

footnotes:
⁷ Which is read on Purim
⁸ To guide the scribe in writing the letters which are suspended from them.
⁹ lit. ‘its truth of’.
¹⁰ Here denotes the Pentateuch.
¹¹ The text corrected by GRA. V has ‘scroll called’. ‘Scroll’ is lit. ‘book’, the word used in Esth. 9, 32. For another reason, cf. Meg. 16b (Sonc. ed., p. 100).


###### Tractate Soferim 13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:3)


עשרת בני המן ומלכי כנען נכתבין אריח ע"ג אריח ולבינה ע"ג לבינה כל בנין כדין לא קאים. מאי כדין למצוה לעיכוב:


The ten sons of Haman¹² and the kings of Canaan¹³ are written in the form of a half-brick over a half-brick and a brick over a brick.¹⁴ (cf. above XII, 8). Such a structure¹⁵ does not endure.¹⁶ What is implied by ‘such’?¹⁷ Does it indicate a recommendation¹⁸ or an indispensable condition?¹⁹

footnotes:
¹² Esth. 9, 7-9.
¹³ Josh. 12, 9-24.
¹⁴ Like the song of Ha’azinu
¹⁵ In which bricks and half-bricks rest one above the other and do not overlap.
¹⁶ And so is symbolic of the fate of Haman’s sons.
¹⁷ The statement ‘such a structure does not last’.
¹⁸ As the arrangement is only a symbol, a deviation from it would not disqualify the scroll.
¹⁹ A deviation would then lead to disqualification. The answer follows in the next Rule.


###### Tractate Soferim 13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:4)


א"ר יוסי בר חנינא אחוה של ר׳ הושעי׳ סבביך ר׳ אבא בר׳ זבדא ושאלינן זה ואמר בשם רב לעיכוב:


R. Jose²⁰ said to R. Ḥanina, the nephew of R. Hoshaiah, ‘Surely you remember that when we were standing in front of the shop of your uncle, R. Hoshaiah, R. Ba’ b. Zabda passed by and we put the question to him, and he replied that the condition was indispensable’.

footnotes:
²⁰ The translation follows GRA and H in agreement with j.Meg. III, 7, 74b. V reads: ‘R. Jose b. Ḥanina, the brother of R. Hoshaiah, R. Abba b. Zabda surrounded thee, and we asked him this, and he said in the name of Rab that the condition was indispensable’.


###### Tractate Soferim 13:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:5)


רבי חייא בריה דרב אדא דיפו ר' ירמיה בשם ר׳ זעירא צריך לאומרן בנפיחה אחת עשרת בני המן [ועשרת עמהן]:


R. Ḥiyya b. R. Adda of Jaffa, quoting R. Jeremiah in the name of R. Ze‘ira, said: The names of the ten sons of Haman and the word *ten*²¹ must be recited in one breath.

footnotes:
²¹ Esth. 9, 10.


###### Tractate Soferim 13:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:6)


אמר רבי יוסי בר אבין בכתבן צריך שיהא איש בראש דפא ואת בסופה בששה שיטין עשרת בסוף דפא שחיץ תגין שמנוהון כהדין סקוטראק נטרי׳ כדי שיפלו ולא תהא להם תקומה. (אסתר ג׳:א׳) אחר הדברים האלה גידל המלך אחשורוש את המן בן המדתא תירגם רב יוסף בתר פתגמיא האלין רבי מלכא אחשורוש ית המן המדתא אגגי בר ביזא בר אפליטוס בר דיוס בר דיזוט בר פרוס בר נידן בר בעלקן בר אנטמירוס בר הורם בר הודורס בר שגר בר נגר בר פרמשתא בר ויזתא בר עמלק בר לחינתיה דאליפז בוכריה דעשו רבי יתיה (הוי וית) [ושוי ית] כורסיה לעיל מכל רברבנוהי ועבדוהי דקדם שבריה גיניניה:


R. Jose b. Abin said: When they²² are written down, the word *’ish*²³, the last word of Esth. 9, 6. must be at the top of the column²⁴ and each *we’eth*²⁵ at the end [of the lines, a total] of eleven lines,²⁶ on the right and *we’eth* on the left, the next nine lines having the name on the right and *we’eth* on the left, while the eleventh line has the name *Wayezatha* on the right and *‘asereth* (*ten*) on the left. and *‘asereth* at the end of the column. Thus, in reading transversely, the columns²⁷ and the ten names), and the left (ten times *we’eth* and *‘asereth*). appear laced and closely corded like transverse lacing [of a web].²⁸ It symbolizes that when [the ten sons] fall there will be no rising for them.
The phrase *’aḥar haddebarim … Hammedatha*²⁹. was translated by R. Joseph as follows:³⁰ ‘After these things did king Ahasuerus promote Haman the son of Hammedatha the Aggagite, son of Seraḥ,³¹ son of Buza, son of Aflutos,³² son of Deosis,³³ son of Padom,³⁴ son of Ma‘adi,³⁵ son of Be‘alkon,³⁶ son of Antamarus,³⁷ son of Ḥarum,³⁸ son of Hadoram,³⁹ son of Shazar,⁴⁰ son of Nagar,⁴¹ son of Parshandatha,⁴² son of Wayezatha, son of ‘Amaleḳ,⁴³ son of the concubine of Eliphaz the firstborn of Esau; he advanced him and set his seat above all his princes and his servants, because pride goeth before destruction’.⁴⁴

²² The names of Haman’s sons.
²³ Man
²⁴ And at the beginning of the first line.

²⁶ The first line consisting of ’ish
²⁷ On the right (consisting of ’ish
²⁸ The reading of V is corrupt and the translation follows GRA, N.Y. and Jastrow.
²⁹ Esth. 3, 1, E.V. After these things did king Ahasuerus promote Haman the son of Hammedatha

³¹ A name derived from a root ‘to stink’. The object of the genealogy is to trace Haman, the symbol of the enemies of Jews throughout their history, through a chain of despicable men to Esau, the brother but enemy of the patriarch Jacob.
³² Buza means ‘contempt’, and Aflutos is probably Plutos, a heathen god.
³³ Dionysus (?).

⁴² This and the following were also names of two of Haman’s sons.
⁴³ The ancestor of the people who were the first to attack Israel after their exodus from Egypt.

###### Tractate Soferim 13:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:7)


אמר רב זעירא וי"ו דויזתא צריך למזקפיה כמדורא דדברות:


R. Ze‘ira said: The *waw* of *Wayezatha*⁴⁵ must project like a boat-pole on the river Libruth.⁴⁶ must be longer than the other letters. The ruling is quoted from Meg. 16b (Sonc. ed., p. 99). The river has not been identified.

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Esth. 9, 9.
⁴⁶ i.e. the waw


###### Tractate Soferim 13:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:8)


מי שהוא אוחז ס"ת נחלקו בדבר שני תנאין חדא אמר פותח ורואה וגולל ומברך וחדא אמר פותח [ורואה] ומברך ומה טעם דכתיב (נחמיה ח׳:ו׳) ובפתחו עמדו כל העם ומה כתיב בתריה ויברך עזרא את ה׳ האלהים הגדול במה גדלו רב אמר גדלו בשם המפורש רבא אמר בברכה גדלו והיכי מברך בעשרה אומרים ברכו את ה' המבורך ביחיד כשהוא משכים לקרוא אומר בא"י אלהינו מלך העולם אשר נתן לנו תורה מן השמים חיי עולמים ממרומים ברוך אתה ה׳ נותן התורה וגולל ואומר ברוך אתה ה׳ אלהינו מלך העולם אשר נתן לנו תורת אמת וחיי עולם נטע בתוכנו ברוך אתה ה׳ נותן התורה:


If a person holds a scroll of the Torah [in order to read it], there is a difference of opinion between two *Tanna’im*.⁴⁷ One declared that he should open it, look [for the passage which is to be read], roll it up and then say the benediction;⁴⁸ but the other declared that he should open it, look [for the required passage] and say the benediction.⁴⁹). What is the reason?⁵⁰ Because it is written, *And when he opened it, all the people stood up;*⁵¹ and what follows? *And Ezra blessed*⁵² *the Lord, the great God*.⁵³ 6. As Ezra *opened* the scroll and *blessed the Lord*, so everyone who reads the Torah must have the scroll open when the benedictions are said. Whereby did he proclaim His greatness?⁵⁴. Rab said: He proclaimed His greatness by pronouncing the Ineffable Name.⁵⁵ Raba said: He proclaimed His greatness by the recital of the prescribed benediction.
What is the form of the benedictions?⁵⁶ In an assembly of ten⁵⁷ one says, ‘Bless ye the Lord Who is to be blessed’.⁵⁸ In the case of an individual, when he rises early to read in the Torah, he says, ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Who hast given us a Torah from heaven, everlasting life from the heights. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who givest the Torah’. Then⁵⁹ he rolls up the scroll⁶⁰ and says, ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Who hast given us a Torah of truth and hast planted everlasting life in our midst. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who givest the Torah’.⁶¹

footnotes:
⁴⁷ On the question whether the prescribed benedictions should be said while the scroll is open or rolled.
⁴⁸ Were one to say it while the scroll is open, people might erroneously assume that the benediction is written in the Torah (Meg. 32a, Sonc. ed., pp. 192f).
⁴⁹ Because nobody could be so ignorant as to be unaware that the benedictions do not form a part of the text of the Torah (Meg. loc. cit.
⁵⁰ That the scroll must be open when the benediction is said.
⁵¹ Neh. 8, 5 referring to the scroll of the Torah.
⁵² i.e. he said the prescribed benedictions.
⁵³ ibid.
⁵⁴ As implied in the words the great God
⁵⁵ The Tetragrammaton.
⁵⁶ Before and after the reading in the Torah.
⁵⁷ A minimum of ten males over the age of thirteen is required for congregational worship.
⁵⁸ And then follow the benedictions given later for an individual in private reading.
⁵⁹ At the conclusion of the reading.
⁶⁰ This is a unanimous ruling. It is upheld even by the Rabbi who maintained that the scroll must be open when the first benediction is said.
⁶¹ The benedictions now in use are shorter (P.B., p. 147).


###### Tractate Soferim 13:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:9)


וכן בנביאים אומרים ברוך אתה ה׳ אלהינו מלך העולם אשר בחר בנביאים טובים ורצה בדבריהם הנאמרים באמת ברוך אתה ה׳ הבוחר בתורה ובמשה עבדו ובישראל עמו ובנביאי האמת והצדק:


Similarly when one reads from the Prophets,⁶² which is read after the conclusion of the prescribed section of the Torah. a benediction is said as follows: ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Who hast chosen good prophets, and hast found pleasure in their words which were spoken in truth. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who hast chosen the Torah and Moses Thy servant, and Israel Thy people, and the prophets of truth and righteousness’.⁶³, the noun being preceded by *he*, the definite article. This is the version adopted by the Sefaradim, while the Ashkenazim omit the article (cf. P.B., p. 149).

footnotes:
⁶² The hafṭarah
⁶³ Wehaẓẓedeḳ


###### Tractate Soferim 13:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:10)


ולבסוף מברך ברוך אתה ה׳ אלהינו מלך העולם צור כל העולמים צדיק בכל הדורות האל הנאמן האומר ועושה מדבר ומקיים כי כל דבריו אמת וצדק. ומיד עומדין העם ואומרים נאמן אתה הוא ה׳ אלהינו נאמנים דבריך נאמן חי וקים שמך וזכרך תמיד ימלוך עלינו לעולם ועד זה אחד ממחלוקת בני מזרח ובני מערב שבני מזרח עונין אותו בישיבה ובני מערב בעמידה ובכל מקום שנחלקו שני תנאים ושני אמוראים ולא בדיק לן הלכתא כחד מינייהו אזלינן בתר מחמיר כל שכן בשבח ותהלה של חי העולמים שהדבר גלוי בלב לשנות הלכה למעשה שנא׳ (משלי ט״ז:ד׳) כל פעל ה׳ למענהו:


And at the end [of the reading of the portion from the Prophets], one says the following benedictions: ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Rock of all worlds, righteous through all generations, O faithful God, Who sayest and doest, Who speakest⁶⁴, without the article, as in the current version (P.B., *loc. cit.*). and fulfillest, all Whose words are truth and righteousness’. The people forthwith stand and say:⁶⁵ ‘Faithful art Thou, O Lord our God, faithful are Thy words, ever faithful, living and enduring are Thy name and remembrance; and do Thou reign⁶⁶ continually over us for ever and ever’. This point⁶⁷ is one of the disagreements between the scholars of the East and West; the former give the response⁶⁸ while sitting and the latter while standing. But wherever two *Tanna’im* or two *’Amora’im* differ, and we are not certain with whom the *halakah* agrees, we follow him who maintains the stricter view. How much more then must we, in regard to the praise⁶⁹ of the Life of all the worlds,⁷⁰, I, p. 85.] which is a matter of the heart,⁷¹ (revealed), M and H *taluï* (dependent). teach our disciples as the rule to be practised that the *halakah* is in agreement with him who upholds the stricter view;⁷² as Scripture states, *The Lord hath made every thing for His own purpose*.⁷³

⁶⁹ The benedictions.
⁷⁰ [On this designation of God, cf. Marmorstein, The Old Rabbinic Doctrine of God
⁷¹ V reads galuï
⁷² Following the emended text of GRA.
⁷³ Prov. 16, 4. There is consequently all the more reason why the stricter view should be followed, viz. that the blessings and praises of God be said while standing.


###### Tractate Soferim 13:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:11)


ואחר כך חוזר המפטיר לענינו ואומר נאמן אתה הוא ה' אלהינו ונאמנים דבריך ודבר אחד מדבריך לא ישוב ריקם כי אל מלך נאמן אתה ברוך אתה ה׳ האל הנאמן בכל דבריו:


After that⁷⁴ the *mafṭir* resumes his recital⁷⁵ and says, ‘Faithful art Thou, O Lord our God, and faithful are Thy words, and not one of Thy words shall return void, for Thou art a faithful⁷⁶ God and King. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God Who art faithful in all Thy words’.

###### Tractate Soferim 13:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:12)


נחם ה׳ אלהינו ציון עירך כי היא בית חיינו ולעלובת נפש תושיע במהרה בימינו ברוך אתה ה' משמח ציון בבניה:


‘Comfort,⁷⁷. The version now in use (P.B., *loc. cit.*) reads ‘have mercy’ instead of ‘comfort’ (GRA emends accordingly), and ‘O Lord our God’ and ‘Thy city’ are omitted. O Lord our God, Zion Thy city, for it is the home of our life, and save her that is grieved in spirit speedily, even in our days. [40*b*] Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who makest Zion joyful through her children.’

###### Tractate Soferim 13:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:13)


שמחנו יי׳ אלהינו כו' שלא תכבה נרו לעולם בימיו תושע יהודה וישראל ישכון לבטח וזה שמו אשר יקראו ה׳ צדקנו (ירמיהו כ״ג:ו׳) ברוך אתה ה׳ מצמיח קרן ישועה לעמו ישראל :


‘Gladden us, O Lord our God,⁷⁸ with Elijah the prophet, Thy servant, and with the kingdom of the house of David, Thine anointed. Soon may he come and rejoice our hearts. Suffer not a stranger to sit upon his throne, nor let others any longer inherit his glory; for by Thy holy name Thou didst swear unto him, that his light should not be quenched for ever. In his days Judah shall be saved, and Israel shall dwell safely; and this is his name whereby he shall be called: the Lord is our righteousness.⁷⁹ Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who causest the horn of salvation to flourish for Thy people Israel.’⁸⁰

⁷⁸ From ‘with Elijah’ to ‘swear unto him’ is represented in V by ‘etc.’ M and H cite the benediction in full.
⁷⁹ ‘In his days … righteousness’ is a quotation from Jer. 23, 6 and is now omitted from the benediction.
⁸⁰ The present custom (in accordance with Pes. 117b, Sonc. ed., p. 603) is to conclude, ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the Shield of David’.


###### Tractate Soferim 13:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/13:14)


על התורה ועל הנביאים ועל יום פלוני הקדוש הזה אשר נתת לנו יי׳ אלהינו לקדושה ולמנוחה לכבוד ולתפארת על הכל ה׳ אלהינו אנו מודים לך ומברכים את שמך תמיד אלהי ישענו ברוך אתה ה' מקדש ישראל ויום פלוני חוץ משבת שאינו מזכיר בחתימה ישראל אלא מקדש השבת בלבד שהשבת קדמה לישראל כדכתיב (שמות ל״א:י״ז) כי ששת ימים עשה ה' את השמים ואת הארץ וביום השביעי שבת וינפש ואומר (שם טז) ראו כי ה׳ נתן לכם (את) השבת שהיתה כבר:


‘For the Torah, [for the divine service],⁸¹ for the prophets and for this holy⁸² … which Thou, O Lord our God, hast given us for holiness and for rest, for honour and for glory. For all these things, O Lord our God, we thank Thee, and continually bless⁸³ Thy name, O God of our salvation. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who sanctifiest Israel and such and such a day.’⁸⁴
An exception is made on the Sabbath when ‘Israel’ is not mentioned in the conclusion but only ‘Who sanctifiest the Sabbath’; because the Sabbath preceded Israel, as it is written, *For in six days the Lord made heaven and earth, and on the seventh day He ceased from work and rested*,⁸⁵ and it is further said, *See that the Lord hath given you the sabbath*,⁸⁶ XVI, 29. The Sabbath was instituted at the creation of the world, and the use of the definite article in the second quotation indicates that it was a well-known institution at the time. The Festivals, on the other hand, were ordained after the people of Israel came into existence. which implies that the Sabbath had already been in existence.

footnotes:
⁸¹ M and H insert this phrase which is included in P.B.
⁸² The ‘Sabbath’ or the name of the Festival is inserted here; and when a Festival coincides with the Sabbath both are mentioned.
⁸³ The P.B. version reads, ‘Thee, blessed be Thy name by the mouth of every living being continually and for ever’.
⁸⁴ For ‘such and such a day’ the name of the Festival is inserted.
⁸⁵ Ex. 31, 17.
⁸⁶ ibid.



## Chapter 14

###### Tractate Soferim 14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:1)


ולמה אמרו כולן כדי לקיים ברכת שבע כנגד ז׳ קוראים וכן אתה אומר בכ"ב פסוקים בנביא כ"א כנגד ז' קרואים מג' ג׳ ואחד כנגד חזן הכנסת:


And why did [the Rabbis] enact all these [benedictions]?¹ as specified in the previous chapter. In order to establish a correspondence between the seven benedictions [of the *hafṭarah*] and the seven readers.² You may also similarly explain the reason for the twenty-two verses read in the [Book of a] Prophet: twenty-two corresponding to the seven readers, each of whom must read [a minimum of] three verses, and one corresponding to the superintendent of the Synagogue services.

footnotes:
¹ For the hafṭarah
² The minimum number who must be called to the reading of the Torah every Sabbath.


###### Tractate Soferim 14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:2)


בד"א בשלא תרגמו ושלא דרשו אבל תרגמו ודרשו המפטיר מפטיר שלשה או חמשה או ששה בנביא ודיו:


All that had been said³ applies only where the text was neither translated nor expounded; but where it was translated or expounded⁴ the *mafṭir* reads three, five or six verses in the Prophet, and this suffices.⁵

footnotes:
³ In the preceding Rule concerning the minimum number of verses to be read in the Prophet.
⁴ So that much of the time of the worshippers was taken up by the translation or exposition.
⁵ The reading is curtailed for the convenience of the congregation.


###### Tractate Soferim 14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:3)


ברות ובשיר השירים באיכה ובמגלת אסתר צריך לומר על מקרא מגילה ואע"פ שכתובה בכתובים:


In the case of Ruth, the Song of Songs, Ecclesiastes,⁶ Lamentations and Esther,⁷. it is necessary to say the benediction, ‘Concerning the reading of the *Megillah*’,⁸ (P.B., p. 276). The present custom is to say the benediction for the scroll of Esther only. The others are read usually from a printed book without a benediction. although it is included in the Hagiographa.

footnotes:
⁶ Inserted by GRA and H.
⁷ These books are read respectively on Pentecost, Passover, Tabernacles, the Fast of Ab and Purim
⁸ The full benediction is: ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Who hast sanctified us by Thy commandments, and hast given us command concerning the reading of the Megillah’


###### Tractate Soferim 14:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:4)


הקורא בכתובים צריך לומר ברוך אתה ה׳ אלהינו מלך העולם אשר קדשנו במצותיו וצונו לקרות בכתבי הקדש:


One who reads in the Hagiographa must say, ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Who hast sanctified us by Thy commandments and hast given us command to read in the Holy Writings’.


###### Tractate Soferim 14:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:5)


במגלת אסתר אמרו שצריך להזכיר בו זמן ולאחר קריאתה צריך הקורא לומר בא"י אמ"ה האל הרב את ריבנו והדן את דיננו והנוקם את נקמתנו והגואלנו והמושיענו מכף כל עריצים ברוך אתה ה׳ האל העוזר ומושיע. כנגד עלבונה של תורה מברכין וי"א כנגד עלבונם של ישראל מברכין:


[The Rabbis] enacted that, as regards [the reading of] the scroll of Esther, the benediction on the ‘season’ must be said;⁹ and after the conclusion of the reading the reader must say, ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, O God Who dost plead our cause, judge our suit and avenge our wrong, Who redeemest and savest us from the hand of all tyrants. Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, Helper and Saviour’.¹⁰ The benediction is said¹¹ which includes this beginning. on account of the humiliation of the Torah, while others maintain that it is said on account of the humiliation of Israel.¹², it must consequently begin again with the usual formula. For ‘Torah’, M and H read ‘the Divine Presence’ affronted by Haman’s plot against God’s people.

footnotes:
⁹ Like the benediction in the preceding note it begins with ‘Blessed art Thou … universe’ and concludes with ‘Who hast kept us in life, and hast preserved us, and enabled us to reach this season’.
¹⁰ Cf. the version in P.B., p. 276.
¹¹ The reason for the benediction is given to explain why it must begin with ‘Blessed art Thou’, although it would appear to be a continuation of the benediction for the Megillah
¹² Whose vindication is celebrated. As it is independent of the Megillah


###### Tractate Soferim 14:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:6)


ועור אמרו שכן צריך ליתן שבח והודאה על הגאולה ועל הפדות וחותם בא"י האל הנקמות המשלם גמול לאויבים ומגן לצדיקים ומושיע עמו מיד שונאיהם ואחר כך מקלס לצדיקים ברוך מרדכי ברוכה אסתר ברוכים כל ישראל ורב אמר צריך לומר ארור המן וארורים בניו אמר רבי פנחס צ"ל חרבונה זכור לטוב:


[The Rabbis] further laid down that it is also necessary to offer praise and thanksgiving [in the benediction] for the redemption and release.¹³ Then one concludes: ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of vengeance, Who dost requite enemies according to their deeds, art a Shield to the righteous, and savest Thy people from the hands of their adversaries’. After that the righteous are lauded: ‘Blessed be Mordecai, blessed be Esther, blessed be all Israel’. Rab, however, declared that it is necessary to say, ‘Accursed be Haman and accursed be his sons’. R. Phinehas said: It is necessary to say, ‘Harbonah be remembered for good’.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹³ From threatened destruction.
¹⁴ He contributed to Haman’s downfall (cf. Esth. 7, 9). For the version now in use, cf. P.B., p. 277.


###### Tractate Soferim 14:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:7)


ר׳ ברכיה רבי ירמיה רבי חייא בשם רבי יוחנן כד הוה מטי ר' (יוחנן) [יונתן] להדין פסוקא (אסתר ב) אשר הגלה נבוכדנצר הוה אמר נבוכדנצר רשיעא שחיט שמוי למזגא לטבא טבתי׳ ולביש בישתיה שנא׳ (משלי י) זכר צדיק לברכה ושם רשעים ירקב:


R. Berekiah, quoting R. Jeremiah who had it from R. Ḥiyya, who reported it in the name of R. Joḥanan, said: When R. Jonathan came to the verse, *Whom Nebuchadnezzar … had carried away*,¹⁵ he used to exclaim, ‘O the wicked Nebuchadnezzar, may his bones be crushed! the good is rewarded¹⁶ according to his goodness and the wicked according to his wickedness, as it is stated, *The memory of the righteous shall be for a blessing; but the name of the wicked shall rot*’.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁵ Esth 2, 6.
¹⁶ lit. ‘to mix’ (drinks), hence ‘to serve drinks’ and, metaphorically, ‘retribution, recompense’.
¹⁷ Prov. 10, 7. This verse justifies the execration of Nebuchadnezzar and (in Rule 6) of Haman.


###### Tractate Soferim 14:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:8)


המפטיר בנביא הוא פורס על שמע באיזה שמע אמרו בשמע של ספר תורה היכי פתח אשרי יושבי ביתך ואח"כ עומד המפטיר ואומר (תהלים פו) אין כמוך באלהים ה' ואין כמעשיך (שמות ט״ו:י״א) מי כמוך באלים ה׳ מי כמוך נאדר בקדש נורא תהלות עושה פלא (תהילים קמ״ה:י״ג-י״ד) מלכותך מלכות כל עולמים וממשלתך בכל דור ודור. ה׳ מלך ה׳ מלך (שמות ט״ו:י״ח) ה' ימלוך לעולם ועד (ישעיהו מ״ב:כ״א) ה׳ חפץ למען צדקו יגדיל תורה ויאדיר (תהילים כ״ט:י״א) ה׳ עוז לעמו יתן ה' יברך את עמו בשלום (נחמיה ט׳:ו׳) אתה הוא [ה'] לבדך אתה עשית את השמים שמי השמים וכל צבאם הארץ וכל אשר עליה הימים וכל אשר בהם ואתה מחיה את כולם וצבא השמים לך משתחוים:


The *mafṭir* recites also the parts of the *Shema‘*.¹⁸ is deemed to be a great honour which compensates for the lesser dignity conferred by the reading of the *hafṭarah*. Of what Shema‘ has this been said?¹⁹ is recited several times during the service. Of the one [recited when] the scroll of the Torah [is taken from the ark].²⁰ How does one begin?²¹ *Happy are they that dwell in Thy house*.²² Then the *mafṭir* stands and recites, *There is none like unto Thee among the gods, O Lord; and there are no works like Thine*.²³ LXXXVI, 8. *Who is like unto Thee, O Lord, among the mighty? Who is like unto Thee, glorious in holiness, fearful in praises, doing wonders?*²⁴ *Thy kingdom is a kingdom for all ages, and Thy dominion endureth throughout all generations*.²⁵ *The Lord is King, the Lord hath reigned, the Lord shall reign for ever and ever*.²⁶) and Ex. 15, 18. *The Lord was pleased, for His righteousness’ sake, to make the teaching great and glorious*.²⁷ *The Lord will give strength unto His people; the Lord will bless His people with peace*.²⁸ *Thou art the Lord, even Thou alone; Thou hast made heaven, the heaven of heavens, with all their host, the earth and all things that are therein, the seas and all that is in them and Thou preservest them all; and the host of heaven worshippeth Thee*.²⁹

###### Tractate Soferim 14:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:9)


מיד נכנס המפטיר ואוחז את התורה ואומר שמע ישראל פסוק הראשון בנעימה ואף העם עונין אותו אחריו:


Thereupon³⁰ the *mafṭir* steps forward,³¹ takes the scroll of the Torah and says, *Hear, O Israel*, chanting the first verse,³². and the people say it after him in response.

###### Tractate Soferim 14:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:10)


וחוזר ואומר אחד אלהינו גדול אדונינו קדוש אחד אלהינו גדול אדונינו קדוש ונורא שמו כנגד שלשה אבות ויש אומרים כנגד שלש קדושות:


[The *mafṭir*] then elevates the Torah scroll and says: ‘One is our God, great is our Lord, holy and revered is His name for ever and ever’.³³ Then he repeats: ‘One is our God, great is our Lord, holy is He. One is our God, great is our Lord, holy and revered is His name’. [This is said three times] corresponding in number³⁴ to the three patriarchs; others hold that it corresponds to the three *kedushshoth*.³⁵

³³ This is the reading of GRA. V, M and H omit the sentence.
³⁴ The three repetitions of ‘one is our God’, etc.

###### Tractate Soferim 14:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:11)


(תהילים ע״א:י״ט) וצדקתך אלהים עד מרום אשר עשית גדולות אלהים מי כמוך (שם קלה) ה׳ שמך לעולם ה׳ זכרך לדור ודור הכל תנו עוז לאלהים ותנו כבוד לתורה (שם לד) גדלו לה' אתי ונרוממה שמו יחדיו. וצריך להגביה את התורה בשמע ישראל ובאלו ייחודין שלשה גדלו לה׳ אתי:


³⁶ *‘Thy righteousness also, O God, reacheth unto high heaven; Thou Who hast done great things, O God, who is like unto Thee?*³⁷ *O Lord, Thy name endureth for ever; Thy memorial, O Lord, throughout all generations*.³⁸ CXXXV, 13. Let all ascribe strength to God and give honour to the Torah. *O magnify the Lord with me, and let us exalt His name together*.’³⁹ XXXIV, 4. The Torah scroll must be elevated at *Hear, O Israel*, at the threefold declaration of the unity of God, and at *O magnify the Lord with me*.⁴⁰

###### Tractate Soferim 14:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:12)


ועוד צריך [לומר] על הכל יתגדל וישתבח ויתפאר ויתרומם ויתנשא ויתעלה ויתהדר ויתקלס (הנכבד והנורא) שמו של מלך מלכי המלכים הקב"ה בעולמות שברא העולם הזה והעולם הבא כרצונו וכרצון כל עמו בית ישראל תגלה ותראה מלכותו עלינו במהרה ובזמן קרוב והוא יבנה ביתו בימינו ויחון פלטתנו ופלטת עמו ישראל ברחמיו וברוב חסדיו בשלום ובחן ובחסד וברחמים המקום (הוא) יעשה עמנו בעבור שמו הגדול ואמרו אמן:


One must also say:⁴¹ ‘Magnified and hallowed,⁴² praised and glorified, exalted and extolled, uplifted, honoured and adored⁴³ above all be the name of the supreme King of kings, the Holy One, blessed be He, in the worlds which He hath created, this world and the world to come, in accordance with His desire and the desire of all His people, the house of Israel. May His kingdom be revealed and made visible unto us speedily and at a near time; and may He build His house in our days, and be gracious unto our remnant and unto the remnant of His people Israel in His mercy and in the abundance of His lovingkindness, in peace and grace, kindness and compassion. May the Omnipresent do all this for us for the sake of His great name; and say ye, Amen’.⁴⁴

footnotes:
⁴¹ In concluding the verses and prayers cited in the preceding Rules.
⁴² This word is included by GRA and H.
⁴³ V adds in parenthesis ‘honoured and revered’ (so also M).
⁴⁴ Cf. the version in P.B., pp. 145f.


###### Tractate Soferim 14:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:13)


(ואח"כ מגביה את התורה ואומר אחד אלהינו גדול אדונינו קדוש ונורא שמו לעולם ועד ומתחיל בנעימה ואומר ה׳ הוא האלהים ה׳ שמו ואחריו עונין אותו העם וחוזר וכופלו ועונין אותו פעמים):


After that he elevates the Torah scroll and says: ‘One is our God, great is our Lord, holy and revered is His name for ever and ever’. Then he begins to chant, ‘The Lord is God, the Lord is His name’. The congregation says it after him in response; then he repeats it twice and the congregation responds twice.⁴⁵

footnotes:
⁴⁵ This Rule is enclosed in round brackets in V. It appears to be a duplicate of Rule 10, but both M and H include it.


###### Tractate Soferim 14:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/14:14)


מיד גולל ספר תורה עד שלשה דפין ומגביהו ומראה פני כתיבתו לעם העומדים לימינו ולשמאלו ומחזירו לפניו ולאחריו שמצוה לכל האנשים ולנשים לראות הכתב ולכרוע ולומר (דברים ד׳:מ״ד) וזאת התורה אשר שם משה לפני בני ישראל (או) (תהילים י״ט:ח׳) תורת ה׳ תמימה משיבת נפש והמפטיר נותנו לחזן הכנסת והוא חוזר התורה לכסות ראשי הקרואים שאין כבוד להיות התורה יחידה וכיוצא בו אינו מן המובחר שיעמוד החזן יחידי לפני התיבה אלא שיעמדו עמו אחד לימינו ואחד לשמאלו כנגד אבות (ויש אומרים פורס את שמע שיאמר יוצר אור וקדוש וטעם לדבר על הברכה שמברך על התורה ועל העבודה) כך היו נקיי הדעת שבירושלים עושין כשהיו מוציאין את התורה ומחזירין היו הולכין אחריה מפני כבודה:


The scroll of the Torah is immediately unrolled a space of three columns and is elevated so as to show the face of the script to the people standing on the right and on the left. Then it is turned round towards the front and towards the rear; for it is a precept for all men and women to see the script,⁴⁶ to bend their knees and exclaim, ‘*And this is the Torah which Moses set before the children of Israel*.⁴⁷ *The Torah of the Lord is perfect, restoring the soul*’.⁴⁸ The *mafṭir* then hands it over to the superintendent of the Synagogue services who returns it to the first⁴⁹ of those who are to be called to the reading, because it is not an honour for the Torah to be left alone.⁵⁰, the superintendent and one of the congregation who is to read a portion to accompany the scroll. Similarly it is not proper for the precentor to stand alone before the reading-desk; so [two persons] should stand with him, one on his right and the other on his left, [the number] corresponding to that of the patriarchs.⁵¹] recites the *Shema‘* with the benediction “Who formest light” (P.B., p. 128) and “Holy” (p. 131) because of the benediction which he says “For the Torah, for the divine service”, etc. (pp. 149f)’. The pure-minded men of Jerusalem acted in this manner: When the Torah scroll was taken out of the ark and when it was returned they followed it as a mark of respect.

⁴⁸ Ps. 19, 8. This verse is not included now.
