⁶⁷ On the ninth of Ab.
⁶⁸ As a mark of mourning for the destruction.
⁶⁹ ‘Bless ye the Lord Who is to be blessed’ which is addressed to the congregation at the beginning of the evening service (cf. P.B., p. 96).
⁷⁰ Which was exclaimed before ‘Bless ye’ but is not now said.



## Chapter 19

###### Tractate Soferim 19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:1)


אבל בתפלת שחרית אחר המזמורים והפסוקים אלו אומר ביוצר ברכו בקול נמוך ואין מזכירין קדוש ואף לא קדיש אבל בתפלת המנחה אומר קדוש וקדיש:


In the morning service,¹, the services of which were treated in the previous chapter. however,² after the psalms and the Scriptural verses, [the reader] in the *Yoẓer* benediction³ says ‘Bless ye’ in a low voice; but neither ‘Holy’⁴, ‘Holy, holy’, etc. (P.B., p. 39). nor *ḳaddish* is to be recited. In the afternoon prayer, however, both ‘Holy’⁵ and *ḳaddish* are said.

footnotes:
¹ Of the ninth of Ab
² The contrast is with the ruling in XVIII, 8 where it was stated that in the evening service ‘Bless ye’ is not said.
³ Cf. P.B., p. 37.
⁴ The ḳedushshah
⁵ P.B., p. 94C.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:2)


ובראש השנה אומר כל העמים תקעו כף (תהילים מ״ז:ב׳) ביום הכפורים ברכי נפשי (שם קד) וממעמעקים קראתיך ה׳ (שם קל) בחג הסוכות נודע ביהודה (שם עו) ביום השמיני למנצח על השמינית (שם ו) ואם אינו בקי בהן אומר אודה ה׳ בכל לבי (שם ט) ודיו:


⁶ And on the New Year we say, *O clap your hands, all ye peoples;*⁷ on the Day of Atonement *Bless the Lord, O my soul*⁸ and *Out of the depths have I called Thee, O Lord;*⁹ on the Festival of Tabernacles *In Judah is God known;*¹⁰ on the Eighth Day [of Solemn Assembly] *For the Leader … on the Sheminith*.¹¹ If one is not conversant with these psalms, he says I *will give thanks unto the Lord with my whole heart*,¹² and that suffices.

footnotes:
⁶ This and the following Rule continue the interrupted regulations on the selection of psalms for various occasions begun in XVIII, 2f.
⁷ Ps. 47.
⁸ Ps. 104.
⁹ Ps. 130.
¹⁰ Ps. 76.
¹¹ Ps. 6.
¹² Ps. 9.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:3)


בפסח בין בתפלה בין בכוס [צריך] להזכיר ביום טוב מקרא קודש הזה ביום חג המצות הזה ובחולו של מועד חג פלוני הזה בשביעי אומר ביום שביעי העצרת הזה ואין מזכיר בו חג לפי שאינו חג בפני עצמו:


On Passover, both in the [*‘Amidah*] prayer¹³ and over the cup of wine¹⁴, P.B., pp. 230f. one must mention ‘On this Festival of holy convocation, this day of the Festival of Unleavened Bread’; and during the intermediate days of the Festival ‘This Festival of Unleavened Bread’.¹⁵ On the seventh day one says¹⁶ and *Ḳiddush*. ‘On the Seventh Day of Solemn Assembly’, but no mention is made of ‘Festival’ since it is not an independent Festival.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹³ P.B., p. 228.
¹⁴ The Ḳiddush
¹⁵ So GRA and H. V and M read ‘this so and so Festival’.
¹⁶ In the ‘Amidah
¹⁷ The seventh day is part of the Passover.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:4)


בחג שבועות אומר ביום טוב מקרא קודש הזה וביום חג השבועות הזה וערבית שחרית ומנחה שוין בתפלות:


On the Feast of Weeks¹⁸ one says ‘On this Festival of holy convocation and this day of the Feast of Weeks’. The evening, morning and afternoon services consist of the same prayers.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ Pentecost.
¹⁹ As on Passover except for the change in the name of the Festival.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:5)


בר"ח (ניסן) צריך להזכיר ביום ראש החדש הזה ובראש השנה בי"ט מקרא קודש הוא ובראש החודש הזה וביום ראש השנה הזה וביום תקיעת שופר הזכרון הזה:


On the New Moon of *Nisan*²⁰ is named because it is the first of the months of the year. one must mention²¹ (P.B., p. 50). ‘On this day of the New Moon’; and on the New Year ‘On this Festival of holy convocation,²² this day of the New Year, this Day of Memorial, a day of blowing the *Shofar*’, concluding with²³ ‘King over all the earth, Who sanctifiest Israel and the Day of Memorial and the Seasons’.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁰ And similarly on the other New Moons. Nisan
²¹ In the ‘Amidah
²² V, M and H add ‘and on this day of New Moon’. GRA omits because this addition is no longer customary.
²³ The benediction (P.B., p. 242).
²⁴ So GRA. The reading in V is confused.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:6)


ביום הכפורים אין מזכירין בו ביום טוב שאין יום טוב ביום צום אלא אומר ביום מקרא קודש הזה ביום צום העשור הזה ביום מחילת העון הזה וחותם מוחל וסולח לעונותינו ולעונות עמו ישראל ברחמים ומכפר על פשעיהם מלך על כל הארץ מקדש ישראל ויום צום הכפורים והזמנים ומקרא קודש ואין מזכירין בו לא מועד ולא שמחה שאין שמחה בלא אכילה:


On the Day of Atonement no mention is made²⁵. of ‘on the Festival Day’, because there is no Festival on a fast day; but one says, ‘On this day of holy convocation, on this day of the Fast of the Tenth,²⁶. on this day of pardoning of iniquity’, concluding with²⁷ ‘pardonest and forgivest our iniquities and the iniquities of Thy people Israel in mercy, and grantest remission for their transgressions, King over all the earth, Who sanctifiest Israel and the day of the Fast of Atonement, and the Seasons and the holy convocation’; but no mention is made of Festival and joy since there is no joy without par-taking of food.

footnotes:
²⁵ In the ‘Amidah
²⁶ The Day of Atonement is observed on the tenth of Tishri
²⁷ Cf. P.B., p. 258.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:7)


וכשם שמקלסין יום ראשון וחולו של מועד כך מקלסין ימים טובים בתפלה ביוצר במנחה בערבית והיכן קילוסו אלהינו ואלהי אבותינו גלה כבוד מלכותך עלינו ואחריו אנא אלהינו שם יעלה ויבוא כו׳ והשיאנו (וכתוב לחיים כו׳) וחתימתו ברוך מקדש עמו ישראל ומועד שמחה והזמנים ומקראי קודש:


As we laud [God] on the first day, the intermediate days and the last day²⁸ of the Festival, so we laud Him [on the other] Festivals in the morning,²⁹ prayer which begins the morning benedictions before the *Shema‘*. afternoon and evening prayers.³⁰. And how is He lauded! ‘Our God and the God of our fathers, do Thou make the glory of Thy kingdom manifest upon us’.³¹ This is followed by, ‘We pray Thee, O Lord our God,³² may … rise and come,³³ etc. Bestow upon us’;³⁴, p. 229. and the conclusion of the prayer is, ‘Blessed art Thou Who sanctifiest Thy people Israel, the Festival of joy, the Seasons and the holy convocation’.

footnotes:
²⁸ Added by GRA; omitted in V and M. The reading of H is: ‘As we laud Him on the New Year and Day of Atonement, so we laud Him on the Festivals’.
²⁹ In the Yoẓer
³⁰ In the ‘Amidah
³¹ Cf. P.B., p. 234.
³² So H. V reads ‘our God name’.
³³ Cf. P.B., p. 228.
³⁴ ibid.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:8)


כשם שחתימתן של ראש השנה ויום הכפורים משונה משאר ימים טובים כך תפלתן ואין מזכירין (זכרונות) בשלשה ראשונות ובשלשה אחרונות אלא בב' ימים טובים של ר"ה וביום הכפורים בלבד ואף באלו בקושי התירו אם חלה ראש השנה להיות בשבת אינו אומר יום תרועה אלא זכרון תרועה לפי שאין תקיעת שופר דוחה את השבת בגבולין משום גזירה:


As the concluding words [of the benedictions] of the New Year and the Day of Atonement are different from those of other Festivals, so are the prayers of these days; and we do not include ‘remembrance’³⁵ either in the first three³⁶ pp. 44f). or last three³⁷ pp. 50-54). [benedictions of the *‘Amidah*] except only on the two Festival days of the New Year and Day of Atonement³⁸ pp. 239, 256. and, even with these, permission was given with difficulty.³⁹ If the New Year falls on a Sabbath, one does not say ‘A day of blowing the *Shofar*’ but ‘a day of remembrance of blowing the *Shofar*’, because the blowing of the *Shofar* does not take precedence of the Sabbath in country towns,⁴⁰ this being a preventive measure.⁴¹ on the Sabbath through a public domain to an instructor to take lessons in the art of blowing it.

footnotes:
³⁵ ‘Remember us unto life’, etc. (P.B., p. 239).
³⁶ ‘Blessed art Thou … the holy God’ (ibid.
³⁷ ‘Accept O Lord … with peace’ (ibid.
³⁸ Cf. ibid.
³⁹ Because, as a rule, no break is allowed in these benedictions.
⁴⁰ Outside Jerusalem. This was permitted in the Temple only (Rashi). According to Maimonides the permissibility extended to all Jerusalem.
⁴¹ One might carry the Shofar


###### Tractate Soferim 19:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:9)


בר"ח ישבו החבורות של זקנים ושל בלווטין ושל תלמידים (ביום הראשון) מן המנחה ולמעלה עד שישקע החמה וצריך בברכת היין לומר בא"י אמ"ה בפה"ג בא"י אמ"ה אשר גידל חורשי הורם ולמדם זמנים חדשים טובים ירח כליל לבנה מינה נבונים סודרי עתים פילס צורינו קיצי רגעים שבם מתקן אותם חדשים ומועדים דכתיב (תהילים ק״ד:י״ט) עשה ירח למועדים שמש ידע מבואו (ואומר (ישעיהו ס״ו:כ״ב) כי כאשר השמים החדשים והארץ החדשה וחותם בא"י מקדש ישראל וראשי חדשים וגו' הודו לה׳ כי טוב ביום הזה בירושלם ששים ושמחים כולנו במקום) אליהו הנביא במהרה יבוא אלינו המלך המשיח יצמיח בימינו את הימים בשנים בבנין בית המקדש ירבו שמחות ויענו העם ויאמרו אמן ירבו בשורות טובות בישראל ירבו ימים טובים בישראל ירבו תלמידי תורה בישראל מקודש החודש מקודש בראש חודש מקודש בזמנו מקודש בעיבורו מקודש בתורה מקודש בהלכה מקודש בעליונים מקודש בתחתונים מקודש בא"י מקודש בציון מקודש בירושלים מקודש בכל מקומות ישראל מקודש בפי רבותינו מקודש בבית הוועד הודו לה׳ כי טוב ואומר וכלכם ברוכים ועל כל ברכה וברכה אומר הודו לה׳ כי טוב חוץ מברכת הלוים:


On the New Moon,⁴² the associations of elders, leaders and students were assembled in session⁴³ from the time of the afternoon service onwards until the sun had set and the moon appeared in the night [sky].⁴⁴ It is necessary to say [after the ceremonial meal] in the benediction over the wine,⁴⁵ ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Who createst the fruit of the vine. Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe, Who hast revealed the secret of the renewal of the moon, Who didst instruct and teach us⁴⁶ the [courses of] the seasons, New Moons and Festivals⁴⁷ and Who didst appoint men of understanding who determine the times [of the New Moons and Festivals]. Thou, our Rock, hast weighed and calculated the minutest divisions of time whereby those New Moons and Festivals are determined, as it is written, *Who appointedst the moon for seasons; the sun knoweth his going down*,⁴⁸ and it also states, *For as the new heavens and the new earth, which I will make*’;⁴⁹ and one concludes with ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who sanctifiest Israel and the New Moons’. [Then one says,] ‘O give thanks unto the Lord for He is good; on this day we all in Jerusalem rejoice and are happy in the All-present. May Elijah the prophet soon come to us; may the king Messiah cause to spring forth in our days the [happy] time like the years⁵⁰ when the Temple will be rebuilt, and may joyous occasions multiply in Israel’; and the people answer and say ‘Amen’. [The prayer is then continued,] ‘May good tidings multiply in Israel, may happy days multiply in Israel, may disciples of the Torah multiply in Israel. Consecrated is the New Moon, consecrated at the beginning of the month, consecrated in its proper time, consecrated in its intercalation,⁵¹ consecrated according to⁵² the Torah, consecrated according to the *halakah*, consecrated in the celestial regions, consecrated in the terrestrial regions, consecrated in the land of Israel, consecrated in Zion, consecrated in Jerusalem, consecrated in all the habitations of Israel, consecrated by the order of our Rabbis, consecrated in the House of Assembly. O give thanks unto the Lord for He is good’. Then [the reader] says, ‘You are all blessed’. After every benediction, moreover, [the reader] says, ‘O give thanks unto the Lord for He is good’, except after the benedictions of the Levites.⁵³

footnotes:
⁴² Which occurred on the 31st day of the preceding month. The New Moon was celebrated either on the 30th day of the preceding month (if witnesses testified in time that they saw the new moon in the evening sky) or on the 31st if no reliable witnesses were forthcoming.
⁴³ V inserts in brackets ‘on the first day’. They assembled in the academy where a ritual feast was held in honour of the declaration and sanctification of the New Moon.
⁴⁴ So GRA. V, M and H omit ‘and the moon … sky’.
⁴⁵ A cup of wine was used for the Grace after meals.
⁴⁶ lit. ‘them’.
⁴⁷ The translation follows the version of GRA. The text in V is confused and varies with that of M and H which also disagree with each other.
⁴⁸ Ps. 104, 19.
⁴⁹ Is. 66, 22.
⁵⁰ So M; V wrongly ‘in the years’. H reads: ‘that he may appoint the days like the years’.
⁵¹ i.e. the addition of the 30th day to the preceding month by declaring the 31st day to be the New Moon and the first day of the new month.
⁵² So M and H. V wrongly ‘in’.
⁵³ Who omitted ‘for He is good’ (cf. its omission in 2 Chron. 20, 21). GRA reads ‘the mourners’ instead of ‘the Levites’ (cf. Rule 12 below). N.Y. also had this reading but considers the phrase ‘except after’, etc., superfluous.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:10)


וכשהוא מקלסו מקלסו בשנים עשר טובי העיר כנגד שנים עשר שבטים ושנים עשר חבירים ושנים עשר חדשים ושנים עשר מזלות:


When he⁵⁴ sings the praises of God he does so in the presence of the twelve notable men⁵⁵ of the town and twelve *ḥaberim*⁵⁶ corresponding to the twelve tribes, the twelve months and the twelve constellations.

footnotes:
⁵⁴ i.e. the one who presides over the assembly which is consecrating the new moon.
⁵⁵ lit. ‘good’.
⁵⁶ Members of an association scrupulously observant of the laws concerning ritual purity. The translation accords with the text of GRA and H.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:11)


ואע"פ שאמרו יעלה ויבא בבונה ירושלים וגומר על כל הכוס ברכת הזימון אותו כוס נותנו לאשתו לקיים מה שנא׳ (יחזקאל מ״ד:ל׳) להניח ברכה אל ביתך ומביא לו כוס אחר ומברך בורא פרי הגפן ואחריו אשר בעגולה כולה וכן אתה אומר בברכת חתנים ובברכת אבלים לפי שאין אומר שתי קדושות על כוס אחד ונהגו רבותינו לומר בבקר בברכת חתנים ובברכת אבלים על הכוס בעשרה ובפנים חדשות כל שבעה וכן בערב קודם סעודה:


Although [in the Grace after meals the prayer] ‘May … rise and come’⁵⁷ has been said in [the benediction concluding with] ‘rebuildest Jerusalem’ and all⁵⁸ the benediction of *zimmun* has been said over the cup⁵⁹ [of wine], that cup being passed on to one’s wife⁶⁰ to fulfil that which is stated, *To cause a blessing to rest on thy house*,⁶¹ nevertheless another cup of wine is brought over which is said the benediction ‘Who createst the fruit of the vine’ and afterwards ‘Who didst reveal’, etc.⁶² The same rule also applies⁶³ to the benediction of bridegrooms and the benediction of mourners,⁶⁴ because two sanctifications⁶⁵ must not be pronounced over the same cup. Our Masters have adopted the practice of saying the benediction of bridegrooms⁶⁶ in the morning over a cup of wine in the presence of ten men; and [in the presence of] new guests all the seven days;⁶⁷ and similarly in the evening after⁶⁸ the meal.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ P.B., p. 282.
⁵⁸ lit. ‘and he finishes’.
⁵⁹ So M and H. V reads ‘all the cup’.
⁶⁰ To partake of its contents.
⁶¹ Ez. 44, 30. Cf. ‘his house is his wife’ (Yoma I, 1, Sonc. ed., p. 1). The wife is the mainstay of the home.
⁶² As in Rule 9 above (GRA). V has the meaningless text: ‘which is a circle all of it’.
⁶³ lit. ‘and so you say’.
⁶⁴ These benedictions, too, must be said over a cup of wine other than the one used for the Grace after meals.
⁶⁵ The benedictions whereby the sanctifications are declared: that for the Grace after meals, on the one hand, and the benedictions mentioned on the other.
⁶⁶ V adds ‘and the benediction of mourners’, which is omitted by M and H. The benediction of mourners is dealt with in Rule 12 below.
⁶⁷ Of the wedding festivities. If no new guests arrive, the benedictions are said only on the first day.
⁶⁸ So GRA. V, M and H read ‘before’.


###### Tractate Soferim 19:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/19:12)


וברכת אבלים בערב לאחר התפלה בפני המתפללים על הכוס בשבת שאין אבילות נוהגת בפרהסיא כר"א בן הורקנוס דאמר ראה שלמה כח של גומלי חסדים ובנה להם לישראל שני שערים אחד לחתנים ואחד לאבלים ולמנודים בשבת היו מתקבצין יושבי ירושלים ועולין להר הבית ויושבין בין שני שערים הללו כדי לגמול חסדים לזה ולזה משחרב בית המקדש התקינו שיהו החתנים והאבלים באים לכנסת כדי לגמול להם חסד חתנים לקלסן ולהלוותם לבתיהן אבלים לאחר שיגמור החזן תפלה של מוסף הולך לו אחורי דלתי של בית הכנסת או בפני הכנסת ומוצא שם האבלים וכל קרוביו ואומר עליהם ברכה ואחר כך אומר קדיש ואין אומר בעלמא דעתיד לחדתא אלא על התלמוד ועל הדרש:


With regard to the benediction of mourners on the Sabbath, when mourning is suspended, it is said after the statutory service in the presence of the worshippers over a cup of wine; for R. Eliezer b. Hyrḳanos said:⁶⁹ Solomon saw the greatness⁷⁰ of those who bestow lovingkindness, and built two gates for Israel, one for bridegrooms and the other for mourners and excommunicated persons. On the Sabbath the inhabitants of Jerusalem used to congregate, ascend the Temple Mount and take their seats between these two gates to show kindness to these persons. Since the Temple was destroyed it was enacted that bridegrooms and mourners should go to the Synagogue so that kindness could be shown to them: to bridegrooms by recounting their praises and escorting them to their homes. To mourners [by comforting them as follows:] After the reader finishes the *musaf*, everyone goes behind the doors of the Synagogue, which are in front of⁷¹, ‘the corner of’.] the Synagogue, and there meets the mourners and all their relatives. The benediction⁷² is said followed by *ḳaddish*. The version ‘In the world that will be renewed in the time to come’⁷³ which is now recited by sons after the interment of a parent (P.B., pp. 321f). is only said after the study of⁷⁴ the Talmud and a Rabbinic discourse.⁷⁵

footnotes:
⁶⁹ This follows GRA. V, M and H read: ‘in the evening after the prayer in the presence of the worshippers over a cup on the Sabbath when mourning in public is suspended according to R. Eliezer b. Hyrḳanos who said’. The text is clearly out of order.
⁷⁰ lit. ‘power’.
⁷¹ So GRA. V omits ‘everyone’ and inserts ‘or’ before ‘in front’. [H has the reading pinnath
⁷² For the mourners.
⁷³ This is an enlarged ḳaddish
⁷⁴ lit. ‘over’.
⁷⁵ lit. ‘exposition’.



## Chapter 20

###### Tractate Soferim 20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:1)


ואין מברכין על הירח אלא במוצאי שבת כשהוא מבושם ובכלים נאים ותולה עיניו כנגדה ומיישר את רגליו ומברך אשר במאמרו ברא שחקים וברוח פיו כל צבאם חוק וזמן נתן להם שלא ישנו את תפקידם פועלי אמת שפעולתו אמת וללבנה אמר שתתחדש באור יקר ועטרת תפארת לעמוסי בטן שהם עתידים להתחדש כמותה לפאר יוצרם על כבוד מלכותו בא"י מקדש ראשי חדשים:


The benediction over the moon must not be said except at the conclusion of the Sabbath when one is in an exhilarated mood¹ and well dressed. [43*a*] He directs his eyes towards the moon, places his feet together and pronounces the benediction ‘Who² didst create the heavens by Thy word and all their hosts by the breath of Thy mouth. Thou didst set for them a fixed law and time that they should not change their ordained task. They are glad and rejoice to perform the will of their Possessor.³ They act loyally, for their⁴ action is truth. To the moon Thou didst say that it should renew itself with a precious light and a diadem of beauty for those who have been sustained from the womb⁵ who, like it, will be renewed in the future and glorify their Creator for the majesty of His kingdom. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who hallowest the New Moons’.⁶

footnotes:
¹ After the happiness of the Sabbath day.
² Preceded, as all benedictions, by ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord our God, King of the universe’.
³ Inserted by GRA and M; omitted by V. H adds it in the textual notes.
⁴ So GRA, M and H. V has ‘for his’.
⁵ A reference to Israel (cf. Is. 46, 3).
⁶ H reads ‘hallowest Israel and the New Moons’; the reading of GRA is ‘Who renewest the months’.


###### Tractate Soferim 20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:2)


ואומר ג"פ סימן טוב סימן טוב [תהיה] לכל ישראל ברוך יוצרך ברוך עושך ברוך קונך ברוך בוראך ורוקד ג׳ רקידות כנגדה ואומר ג"פ כשם שאני רוקד כנגדך ואיני נוגע ביך כך אם ירקדו אחרים כנגדי [להזיקני] לא יגעו בי תפול עליהם אימתה ופחד ולמפרע אמן אמן סלה הללויה ואומר לחבירו ג"פ שלום וילך לביתו בלב טוב והדא היא לכל מפסיקין לראשי חדשים:


Then one says three times, ‘An auspicious omen, an auspicious omen, shall it be for all Israel. Blessed be He Who formed thee, blessed be Thy Maker, blessed be thy Possessor, blessed be thy Creator’. Turning in the direction of the moon,⁷ he jumps three times and exclaims three times, ‘As I jump towards thee but do not⁸ touch thee, so if others should jump against me to hurt me may they not touch me. Let terror and dread fall upon them and may this be retrospective, Amen, Amen, Selah. Praise ye the Lord’. He then says ‘Peace’ three times to his fellow and goes home with a cheerful heart. That is [the conclusion of the regulations] concerning the interruptions for New Moons.⁹

footnotes:
⁷ lit. ‘towards it’.
⁸ M reads ‘and cannot’ which is the reading in the ritual.
⁹ Cf. XVII, 2f where the regulations for the New Moon began and, with many interruptions, insertions and amplifications, continued to the end of this Rule.


###### Tractate Soferim 20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:3)


כיצד מפסיקין לחנוכה אמרו בעשרים וחמשה בכסליו מדליקין נר חנוכה ואסור להדליק בנר ישן ואם אין לו אלא ישן מלבנו באור יפה יפה ואין מגביהין אותו ממקומו עד שיכבה:


What are the regulations respecting *Ḥanukkah?*¹⁰’, a reference to XVII, 2 where *Ḥanukkah* was first introduced in this set of regulations with the statement, ‘The sequence … is interrupted for … *Ḥanukkah’*. The Rabbis said: On the twenty-fifth day of *Kislew*¹¹ the *Ḥanukkah* lamp is kindled,¹² and it is forbidden to use an old lamp;¹³ but if a person has only an old lamp, he must thoroughly heat it in fire;¹⁴ nor may [the *Ḥanukkah* lamp] be moved¹⁵ from its place before it is extinguished.¹⁶ light, commemorating the miracles which led up to the celebration (cf. Rule 5), is sacred, is only to be looked at and must not be used for any other purpose.

footnotes:
¹⁰ lit. ‘how do we interrupt for Ḥanukkah
¹¹ The ninth Heb. month on the twenty-fifth of which the Festival begins.
¹² This takes place on the preceding evening, the night belonging to the following day.
¹³ If it is an earthen lamp, because the burnt oil renders it unsightly.
¹⁴ To burn away the old oil, after which the lamp may be used.
¹⁵ lit. ‘lifted’.
¹⁶ Carrying the lamp about while it is burning might cause suspicion that one is making a profane use of it; but the Ḥanukkah


###### Tractate Soferim 20:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:4)


מצות הדלקתו משקיעת החמה עד שיכלה הרגל מן השוק ואע"פ שאין ראייה לדבר זכר לדבר (שמות י״ג:כ״ב) לא ימיש עמוד הענן יומם ואם הדליקו ביום אין נאותין ממנו ואי׳ מברכין עליו שכך אמרו אין מברכין על הנר עד שיאותו לאורו ואין חוששין לפתילתו להחליפו עד שיכלה:


The commandment to kindle it extends from sunset until the last person has left¹⁷ the street.¹⁸ And, although there is no actual proof,¹⁹ there is an allusion to it: *The pillar of cloud by day* [*and the pillar of fire by night*,] *departed not*.²⁰ lamp, was intended to shed light, appeared in the night only If it was lit in daytime, one must derive no benefit from it, nor may the benediction be said over it; for so [the Rabbis] said: No benediction is to be pronounced over the lamp before one enjoys its light. There is no need, however, to be concerned about changing the wick before it has been used up.

footnotes:
¹⁷ lit. ‘the foot ceases from’.
¹⁸ The light is intended to serve as a public demonstration, but at such a late hour there are no passers-by to see it.
¹⁹ That the lighting must take place after sunset.
²⁰ Ex. 13, 22, which indicates that the pillar of fire which, like the Ḥanukkah


###### Tractate Soferim 20:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:5)


נר חנוכה מצוה להניחו בפתח הסמוך לרה"ר שתהא מזוזה בימין ונר חנוכה משמאל לקיים מה שנאמר (ש"ה ז) מה יפית ומה נעמת מה יפית במזוזה ומה נעמת בנר חנוכה ונרות כב"ה משום מעלין בקדש ולא מורידין כעין ימים היוצאין:


It is a religious precept to place the *Ḥanukkah* lamp by the door which is near to the public domain,²¹ in such a manner that the *mezuzah* should be on the right hand and the *Ḥanukkah* lamp on the left, to fulfil what is stated, *How fair and how pleasant art thou*²²—*how fair* with the *mezuzah* and *how pleasant* with the *Ḥanukkah* lamp. The number²³ of lights [for each night] is in accordance with the ruling of the School of Hillel,²⁴ because in sacred matters one should proceed to a higher grade²⁵ but not descend to a lower one,²⁶ [and also because the number of lights should] correspond to the days [of the Festival] as they pass.

footnotes:
²¹ So that the light is seen from the street.
²² Cant. 7, 7.
²³ Inserted by GRA, wanting in V, M and H.
²⁴ One light on the first night, two on the second, and so on, until eight lights burn on the eighth and last night of the Festival.
²⁵ Adding lights on the succeeding nights.
²⁶ As would happen if the number were to be reduced from eight on the first night to one on the last in accordance with the view of Beth Shammai.


###### Tractate Soferim 20:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:6)


וכיצד מברכין ביום ראשון המדליק מברך שלש והרואה מברך שתים בא"י אשר קדשנו במצותיו וצונו להדליק נר ואומר הנרות האלו אנו מדליקין על הישועות ועל הניסים ועל הנפלאות אשר עשית לאבותינו ע"י כהניך הקדושים וכל (מצות) שמונת ימי חנוכה הנרות האלו קודש ואין לנו רשות להשתמש בהן אלא לראותן בלבד כדי להודות לשמך על נפלאותיך ועל נסיך ועל ישועתך בא"י שהחיינו ואומר שעשה נסים לאבותינו אלו למדליק אבל לרואה אינו אומר ביום ראשון אלא שתים שהחיינו ושעשה מכאן ואילך [המדליק] מברך להדליק ושעשה והרואה אומר (אשר עשה) [שעשה] נסים:


In what manner are the benedictions²⁷ lights. said? On the first day, the person who kindles [the lamp] says three benedictions while the onlooker says two. The person who kindles says, ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord,²⁸ Who hast sanctified us by Thy commandments and hast given us command to kindle the light of *Ḥanukkah*’. Then²⁹ he says, ‘We kindle these lights on account of the deliverances and the miracles and the wonders which Thou didst work for our fathers, by means of Thy holy priests.³⁰ During all the eight days of *Ḥanukkah* these lights are sacred, and it is not permitted to make any profane use of them but we are only to look at them, in order that we may give thanks unto Thy name for thy wonders, Thy miracles and Thy deliverances’. [The third benediction is] ‘Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who hast kept us in life’.³¹ [Before this] he says, ‘Who wroughtest miracles for our fathers’.³² These benedictions are for the person who kindles the lights, but the onlooker says on the first day only two benedictions: ‘Who hast kept us in life’ and ‘Who wroughtest miracles’. From that day onwards the person who kindles the lights says, ‘To kindle’ and ‘Who wroughtest’, while the onlooker only says ‘Who wroughtest miracles’.

footnotes:
²⁷ Over the Ḥanukkah
²⁸ Inserting after ‘Lord’, as in all benedictions, ‘our God, King of the universe’ (P.B., p. 274).
²⁹ After the three benedictions: the one just given and the two mentioned below.
³⁰ The Maccabees who were a priestly family.
³¹ Concluding ‘and hast preserved us, and enabled us to reach this season’ (P.B., p. 274).
³² Beginning, as usual, ‘Blessed art … universe’ and concluding ‘in the days of old at this season’ (P.B., p. 274).


###### Tractate Soferim 20:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:7)


החזן צריך לומר אתה קדוש בשמנה עשר של חנוכה וכן בראשי חדשים וכן בחולו של מועד מפני שכתוב בהן עולת תמיד ותני רבי חייא כל יום שאין בו מוסף אין בו קדוש חוץ מן החנוכה שאע"פ שאין בה מוסף יש בה קדוש מפני (מה) שיש בה הלל וי"א אף בפורים מפני שיש בה מגילה:


The precentor must say, ‘Thou art holy’³³. It appears that, instead of beginning with ‘We will sanctify’ (P.B., p. 45) or ‘We will reverence’ (P.B., p. 160), there was a custom to begin with ‘Thou art holy’ on Sabbath and Festivals. Rule 7 extends this practice to some of the less important days as specified below. It has also been suggested to read, instead of *’attah ḳadosh* (‘Thou art holy’) *’eth haḳḳedushshah* (‘the *ḳedushshah*’). on the eight days³⁴ of *Ḥanukkah*, and similarly on New Moons and on the intermediate days of a Festival,³⁵ because *beside*³⁶ *the continual burnt-offering*³⁷ 24 (on the intermediate days of Passover); and *ibid.* XXIX, 19, 22, 25, 28, 31, 34 (on those of Tabernacles). is written in connection with them. And R. Hiyya learnt: On any day on which there is a musaf *ḳedushshah* is said except on *Ḥanukkah* on which *ḳedushshah* is said although there is no *musaf* on it, because *Hallel* is included [in the service]. Some maintain [that it is said] on *Purim* also, because the scroll of Esther is read on it.

footnotes:
³³ i.e. the ḳedushshah
³⁴ So GRA. V, M and H read ‘in the eighteen [benedictions]’.
³⁵ viz. Passover and Tabernacles.
³⁶ Inserted by GRA, omitted by V, M and H.
³⁷ Num. 28, 15 (on the New Moon); ibid.


###### Tractate Soferim 20:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:8)


ואומרים בהודייה והודאת פלאות ותשועת כהנים אשר עשית בימי מתתיהו בן יוחנן כהן גדול וחשמונאי ובניו וכן עשה עמנו ה' אלהינו ואלהי אבותינו נסים ונפלאות ונודה לשמך לנצח בא"י הטוב ונסים מרדכי ואסתר מזכירין אותן בהודייה ושניהן נזכרין בברכת המזון:


In the benediction for Thanksgiving³⁸ we include [on *Ḥanukkah*] ‘and thanks for the wonders and salvation of Thy priests which Thou hast wrought in the days of Mattathias, son of Joḥanan, High Priest, and the Hasmoneans his sons. So also, O Lord our God and God of our fathers, perform for us miracles and wonders, and we will give thanks unto Thy name for ever. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who art all-good’.³⁹ The miracles [of the days] of Mordecai and Esther are also mentioned [on *Purim*] in the benediction for Thanksgiving. Both⁴⁰ and *Purim*. are likewise mentioned in the Grace after meals.⁴¹

footnotes:
³⁸ Beginning ‘We give thanks’ (P.B., p. 51).
³⁹ The present version of this form of thanksgiving is longer and differs in wording (P.B., p. 52).
⁴⁰ Those for Ḥanukkah
⁴¹ In the second benediction (P.B., p. 281).


###### Tractate Soferim 20:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:9)


וגומרין את הלל כל שמנת ימי חנוכה בג׳ פרקים הראשונים אין משיבין ואין צ"ל לשאול בשנים האחרונים שואלין מפני היראה [ומשיבין] מפני השלום ומפני הכבוד ואלו הן שלשה ראשונים הללו עבדי ה׳ בצאת ישראל אהבתי שנים האחרונים הללו את ה׳ הודו לה׳ וצריך לברך בתחילתן ולקרותן בנעימה דתני ר׳ שמעון בן יהוצדק ימים שמנה עשרה ולילה אחד יחיד גומר בהן את ההלל ואלו הן שמנת ימי חנוכה וי"ט של עצרת וי"ט הראשון של פסח ולילו ובגולה עשרים ואחד יום ושתי לילות מצוה מן המובחר לקרות הלל בשני לילות של גליות ולברך עליהן ולאומרן בנעימה לקיים מה שנאמר (תהילים ל״ד:ד׳) ונרוממה שמו יחדיו וכשהוא קורא אותו בביתו אינו צריך לברך שכבר בירך ברבים:


The whole *Hallel*⁴² is recited during all the eight days of *Ḥanukkah*. During [the recital of] the first two⁴³ chapters one may not return greetings, and there is no need to say that one must not give greetings. During [the recital of] the last two chapters one may give greetings out of fear;⁴⁴ during the two middle chapters⁴⁵ [greetings may be given] for the sake of peace or out of respect. The following are the first two chapters: *Praise, O ye servants of the Lord*⁴⁶ and *When Israel went forth*.⁴⁷ CXIV. V, M and H adds Ps. 115 as the third. The two middle ones are: *Not unto us*⁴⁸ CXV; ‘the two middle … *us’* is the reading of GRA. and *I love*.⁴⁹ CXVI. The last two are: *O praise the Lord*⁵⁰ CXVII. and *O give thanks unto the Lord*.⁵¹ CXVIII. It is also necessary to say a benediction⁵²’ (P.B., p. 219). before them and read them melodiously. For R. Simeon b. Jehoẓadaḳ said:⁵³ On eighteen days and one night the individual reads the whole *Hallel* and they are: the eight days of *Ḥanukkah*, the eight days of Tabernacles,⁵⁴ the Festival of Solemn Assembly, the first Festival day of Passover and the night preceding it.⁵⁵ was recited during the sacrifice of the Paschal lamb on the eve of Passover.] In the Diaspora⁵⁶ [the whole *Hallel* is read on] twenty-one days and two nights. The most proper way of performing the commandment is to read the *Hallel* psalms on the two nights [of Passover] in the Diaspora in the Synagogue,⁵⁷ ritual. ‘In the Synagogue’ is inserted by GRA but omitted by V, M and H. to say the benediction over them, and to recite them melodiously to fulfil what is stated, *And let us exalt His name together*.⁵⁸ When, however, one reads [the *Hallel*] at home,⁵⁹. there is no need to say the benediction because it has already been said in the congregational service.⁶⁰ in the home.

⁴⁵ The reading of GRA. V and M have ‘and return greetings for the sake of peace and out of respect’. H omits the text of V and GRA.

###### Tractate Soferim 20:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:10)


ואחר כך מוציאין ספר וקורין בנשיאים ויהי המקריב ביום הראשון וכן לכל יום יומו עד יום השמיני ובשבת שבתוכו קוראו ויהי ביום כלות משה עד כן עשה את המנורה וכן ביום השמיני עד וזה מעשה המנורה כל הפסוק ומפטיר בותשלם כל המלאכה (מלכים א ז׳:נ״א):


A scroll⁶¹ is then taken out⁶². and we read in the section of the princes,⁶³ *And he that presented his offering the first day was;*⁶⁴ 12-17. and likewise for each day [the corresponding section of the offering] of that day,⁶⁵ day corresponding to the respective number of the days of the offerings of the princes. until the eighth day. On the Sabbath [during *Ḥanukkah*] one reads, *And it came to pass on the day that Moses made an end*⁶⁶ up to *so he made the candlestick*.⁶⁷ VIII, 4. On the eighth day he reads from *On the eighth day*⁶⁸ 54. through all the sections⁶⁹ until *so he made the candlestick*. On the Sabbath⁷⁰ (Num. 8, 4) all the verse’. H has a different text. one also reads as the *hafṭarah, Thus all the work … was finished*.⁷¹

###### Tractate Soferim 20:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:11)


(אין) מקדימין בשמנת ימי חנוכה לומר ביום השני ביום השלישי ביום הרביעי ביום החמישי ביום הששי ביום השביעי ביום השמיני כדי לגמור עשרה פסוקים:


During the eight days of *Ḥanukkah* we read the following one day earlier:⁷² *On the second day*,⁷³ *On the third day*,⁷⁴ 24-29. *On the fourth day*,⁷⁵ 30-35. *On the fifth day*,⁷⁶ 36-41. *On the sixth day*,⁷⁷ 42-47. *On the seventh day*,⁷⁸ 48-53. *On the eighth day*,⁷⁹ 54-59. so as to complete ten verses.⁸⁰.

###### Tractate Soferim 20:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/20:12)


בזמן שחל ר"ח טבת להיות באחד בשבת ביום ראשון קורין בענין ר"ח תחלה שלשה והרביעי של חנוכה שכל התדיר מחבירו תדיר קודם והוא קודם את חבירו אבל ביום שני אינו כן אלא קורים שלשה בשל חנוכה והרביעי בשל ר"ח שאין חשבון חודש מיום שני אלא בזמן שהשנים כסדרן דר׳ יצחק סחורה שאל את ר׳ יהושע נפחא ר"ח טבת שחל להיות בשבת במה קורין לענין כלות שלמה והמפטיר בשל שבת ור"ח חל להיות בחול קורין ביום הראשון שלשה [בשל] ר"ח [והרביעי] בשל חנוכה [שכל] התדיר מחבירו (תדיר) קודם את חבירו והואיל וקרא תחלה בשל ר"ח ביום השני קורא ג׳ לחנוכה והרביעי בשל ר"ח ובראשי חדשיכם עד שישלים (כל השיטה) ולמה כן שלא בא הרביעי אלא מחמת מוסף של ר"ח הרי מפסיקין לחנוכה:


When the New Moon of *Ṭebeth*⁸¹. falls on a Sunday, three persons read on the first day⁸² in the passage of the New Moon first, while the fourth reads the passage of *Ḥanukkah*, because that which is more frequent takes precedence,⁸³ once a year. and so [the reading for the New Moon] precedes the other. On the second day, however, the order is not the same, but three persons read the passage of *Ḥanukkah* while the fourth reads that of the New Moon, because on the previous day the section of the New Moon had been read first.⁸⁴ [This is the procedure] because R. Isaac Seḥorah asked R. Isaac Nappaḥa, ‘Which do we read [first]⁸⁵ when the New Moon of *Ṭebeth* falls on a Sabbath?’ [He replied,] ‘In the section of *kalloth Mosheh*,⁸⁶ (Num. 7, 1). The extent of the reading is given in Rule 10 above. while the *mafṭir* reads⁸⁷ the passages relating to the Sabbath and the New Moon.⁸⁸ If it falls on a week-day, on the first day [of the New Moon] three persons read [the passage] for the New Moon, the fourth reading that of *Ḥanukkah;* because that which is more frequent takes precedence. As, however, the section of the New Moon was read first,⁸⁹ three persons are to be called up on the second day for the reading of the *Ḥanukkah* section,⁹⁰. while the fourth reads⁹¹ the section of the New Moon, viz. *And in your new moons* to the end of the section.⁹² Why is this so?⁹³ Because it is only on account of the *musaf* of New Moon⁹⁴ there is no *musaf*. This argument cannot be applied to the first day of New Moon because it is the second day which begins the month, the first being the last day of the old month. that a fourth person is added.⁹⁵ Behold [we have concluded the regulations respecting] the interruption which is made for the sake of *Ḥanukkah*.⁹⁶

footnotes:
⁸¹ Which always occurs during Ḥanukkah
⁸² Of the New Moon, which is observed sometimes on one day and sometimes on two.
⁸³ The New Moon occurs every month, Ḥanukkah
⁸⁴ So GRA. V, M and H read: ‘because the reckoning of the month is from the second day only when the years are regular’.
⁸⁵ In the second and third scrolls of the Torah. In the first the regular portion of the week is read.
⁸⁶ E.V. Moses had made an end
⁸⁷ In the third scroll.
⁸⁸ Num. 28, 9-15.
⁸⁹ On the first day of New Moon.
⁹⁰ In the first scroll. Only two scrolls are taken out on the week-days of Ḥanukkah
⁹¹ In the second scroll.
⁹² Num. 28, 11-15.
⁹³ Why is the order of the reading reversed on the second day as compared with the first?
⁹⁴ On the other week-days of Ḥanukkah
⁹⁵ Rule 12 incorporates nearly the whole of XII, 7.
⁹⁶ Cf. XVII, 2f.



## Chapter 21

###### Tractate Soferim 21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:1)


מפסיקים לפורים כמנהג רבותינו שבמערב להתענות שלשה ימי צום מרדכי ואסתר פרודות ולאחר פורים שני וחמישי ושני:


[Reverting to the sequence of the readings which] is interrupted for *Purim*.¹ It is² the custom of our Rabbis in the West to afflict themselves by fasting the three days of the fast of Mordecai and Esther³ intermittently, viz. after *Purim* on the Monday, Thursday and Monday.

footnotes:
¹ Cf. XVII, 2 where the subject was begun. Having been suspended in favour of other Festivals, it is here resumed.
² So GRA and H. V and M read ‘according to’.
³ Cf. Esth. 4, 16.


###### Tractate Soferim 21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:2)


ולמה אין מתענין אותן בחדש ניסן מפני שבאחד בניסן הוקם המשכן וי"ב נשיאים הקריבו קרבנם לי"ב יום יום לכל שבט ושבט וכל א׳ היה עושה ביומו י"ט וכן לעתיד לבא עתיד המקדש להבנות בניסן לקיים מה שנאמר (קהלת א׳:ט׳) אין כל חדש תחת השמש:


And why do we not fast in the month of *Nisan?* Because on the first of *Nisan* the Tabernacle was erected⁴ and twelve princes offered their sacrifices during [the first] twelve days, a day for each tribe,⁵ and everyone held a festival on his day.⁶ and the fifteenth, on which Passover begins, only two days intervene; therefore they, too, are regarded as festival days. The Passover ending on the twenty-first (in the land of Israel) or on the twenty-second (in the Diaspora), only eight or nine days of the month remain. As by far the greater part of the month was made up of festival days, the entire month is treated as a festival season in which fasting is prohibited. Furthermore, in the time to come the Sanctuary will be rebuilt in *Nisan*, to confirm what is stated, *There is nothing new under the sun*.⁷

footnotes:
⁴ Cf. Ex. 40, 2.
⁵ Cf. Num. 7, 1ff.
⁶ Between the twelfth of Nisan
⁷ Eccl. 1, 9.


###### Tractate Soferim 21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:3)


לפיכך אין אומרין תחנונים כל ימי ניסן ואין מתענין עד שיעבור ניסן אלא הבכורות שמתענין בערב הפסח והצנועין בשביל המצה כדי שיכנסו בה בתאוה והתלמידים מתענין בו שני וחמישי מפני חילול השם ומפני כבוד ההיכל שנשרף:


For this reason⁸ no supplications⁹ are offered on any of the days of *Nisan* and there is no fasting until *Nisan* has passed. The only exception is [the fast of] the firstborn who fast on the eve of Passover.¹⁰ The very pious also fast on that day on account of the unleavened bread, in order that they shall begin to eat it¹¹. Lit. ‘that they shall enter it’. with relish.¹² Scholars fast on Mondays and Thursdays [throughout the year] on account of the desecration of the name of God¹³ and for the honour of the Temple which was burnt.

footnotes:
⁸ That most of the month contained festival days.
⁹ This refers in particular to the prayer, ‘And He, being merciful’, etc. (P.B., pp. 57-65).
¹⁰ In thanksgiving for the deliverance of the Israelite firstborn on Passover eve when Egypt’s firstborn perished (Ex. 12, 29).
¹¹ On the evening preceding the fifteenth of Nisan
¹² In honour and appreciation of the commandment.
¹³ By the heathens who destroyed Jerusalem.


###### Tractate Soferim 21:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:4)


בד"א בצנעה אבל להזכיר בציבור אסור עד שיעבור ניסן. ובאחד באדר משמיעין על השקלים ולמה באחד באדר שהיה צפוי וגלוי וידוע לפני מי שאמר והיה העולם שיהיה המן עתיד לשקול על ישראל לפיכך הקדים ואמר למשה (שהיו ק׳) שקלי ישראל קודמין להמן וצריכין ישראל לתת שקליהם לפני שבת זכור ואסור לומר עליהם לשם כופר אלא לשם נדבה צריכין להספיק מים ומזון לאחיהם עניים משום קובלין ומתנות לאביונים ויש שמספיקין לחם ויין ויש שמספיקין לחם ודגים ומ"מ לא יפחות משתי מתנות אפילו חטים ופולים:


To what does the foregoing apply? To fasting in private, but it is forbidden to proclaim a public fast until *Nisan* has passed.
On the first day of *Adar* a public proclamation is made regarding the shekels.¹⁴ Why on the first of *Adar?* Because it was foreseen, revealed and known before Him Who spoke and the world came into being that Haman would in time to come weigh [shekels] against Israel.¹⁵ He, therefore, forestalled him and instructed Moses that the shekels of Israel should precede Haman’s. It is required that Israel shall give their shekels before [43*b*] the Sabbath of Remember;¹⁶ when the *mafṭir* reads the passage beginning *Remember* (Deut. 25, 17-19). and it is forbidden to say of them¹⁷ that they are ‘for a ransom’ [but must be given] for a freewill-offering. From the proceeds water and food should be supplied for their poor brethren on account of a sacrifice¹⁸ with GRA. V has ‘on account of complaints’ (*ḳoblin*). [M has *ḳislin* which Krauss, *Lehnwörter*, p. 531, renders ‘feast’.] and gifts to the poor.¹⁹ Some supply bread and wine, others supply bread and fish; in any event not less than two gifts²⁰ speaks of *gifts*, in the plural, which indicates not less than two. should be given, although they consist only of wheat and beans.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Every adult had the obligation to give one half-shekel as a contribution to the Temple.
¹⁵ Cf. Esth. 3, 9.
¹⁶ The Sabbath before Purim
¹⁷ The half-shekels.
¹⁸ Reading ḳorban
¹⁹ Cf. Esth. 9, 22.
²⁰ Since Esth. loc. cit.


###### Tractate Soferim 21:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:5)


בי"ד ובט"ו יושבין שולחנות של חבירים בכפרים בי"ד ובכרכים בט"ו בו:


²¹ (“associates”, i.e. men who observed levitical purity) were set up (for changing foreign currency into half-shekels); in the villages on the fourteenth and in the walled cities on the fifteenth’. In villages and towns [the *Megillah*] is read on the fourteenth [of *Adar*], and in walled cities²² on the fifteenth of the month.

footnotes:
²¹ The translation of this Rule follows the reading of GRA. V, M and H read: ‘On the fourteenth and fifteenth of Adar, tables of Ḥaberim
²² Those that, like Jerusalem, had been walled in the days of Joshua the son of Nun (cf. Meg. I, 1).


###### Tractate Soferim 21:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:6)


קורין בויבא עמלק ואע"פ שאין בה [אלא] תשעה פסוקין אין מתחילין בוישמע יתרו אלא ראשון קורא שלשה פסוקים ויבא עמלק ויאמר משה ויעש יהושע. ושני קורא והיה כאשר ירים משה וידי משה ויחלש יהושע שלישי קורא ויאמר ה׳ ויבן משה ויאמר כי יד על כס יה מלחמה לה׳ ומניחין ספר תורה במקומו ואומר (תהילים קי״ג:ב׳) יהי שם ה׳ מבורך מעתה ועד עולם וקדיש (שאין לך קריאה שטעון קדיש עד שיחזיר אלא של שבת בלבד מפני המפטיר ועל הנביא לאחר שמניחין ס"ת במקומו אומר קדיש וקדיש למה אלא ללמד) שאין אומרי׳ קדיש לא בראשי חדשים ולא בתעניות ולא בשני ובחמישי ולא בחולו של מועד ולא בשמונת ימי חנוכה ולא בפורים עד שמחזירים ספר תורה למקומו בשעה שהעם עומדים והן עונין אמן יהא שמיה רבה בכוונה בעמידה :


We read²³ in the morning service. from *Then came Amalek;*²⁴ and although it consists of only nine verses,²⁵ we do not begin²⁶ *Now Jethro … heard*,²⁷. but the first [to be called up] reads three verses, viz. *Then came Amalek … And Moses said … So Joshua did;*²⁸ XVII, 8-10. the second reads *And it came to pass, when Moses held up his hand … But Moses’ hands … And Joshua discomfited;*²⁹ 11-13. the third reads *And the Lord said … And Moses built … And he said: The hand upon the throne of the Lord: the Lord will have war*.³⁰ 14-16. The scroll of the Torah is then put back in its place, and *Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth and for ever*³¹ and *ḳaddish* are said;³² until after he restores [the scroll in the ark] except that of the Sabbath alone on account of the *mafṭir* and over the Prophet. After the scroll of the Torah is put in its place one says *ṭaddish;* and why *ṭaddish?* Only to teach’. GRA transposes this to the end of the Rule in an emended version. because neither on New Moons, nor fast days, nor Mondays and Thursdays, nor on the intermediate days of a Festival, nor the eight days of *Ḥanukkah*, nor *Purim* is *ḳaddish* said until after the Torah scroll had been restored to its place, while the people stand and respond ‘Amen, let His great name’, etc.,³³ with devotion and [still] standing. Furthermore, you have no reading that requires a *ḳaddish* before [the scroll] is put back except that of Sabbath and Festivals alone, [when it is said] before the *hafṭarah* is read in the Prophet.

²⁸ ibid.

###### Tractate Soferim 21:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:7)


רב הונא אמר שלש קריות שבתורה לא יפחתו להם מעשרה פסוקים חזקיה אמר כנגד עשרת הדברות והתנינן ביום הראשון [בראשית] ויהי רקיע דהא לית בהון אלא תמניא רב אידי אמר איתפלגין בה כהנא ואשי חד אמר חוזר וחד אמר חותך מאן דאמר חוזר חוזר לשני פסוקים ומאן דאמר חותך ויהי ערב ויהי בקר פסוק בפני עצמו הוא והתנינן בשני יהי רקיע ויקוו המים מאן דאמר חוזר חוזר לשני פסוקים ומאן דאמר חותך אפילו חותך לית בהון התיב רבי פוליפא בר רבי פריט קמית ר׳ יונה הרי פרשת עמלק אמר לא שנויה היא היא סדרה של יום התיב רבי אליעזר ברבי מקום המפטיר בנביא לא יפחות מעשרים ואחד פסוקים (מדלגין בנביא ואין מדלגין בתורה שאפילו אם דלג פסוק אחד ולא קראו והחזיר את התורה ואמר קדיש חוזר ופותח ומברך וקורא הוא ושנים אחרים):


R. Huna said: For three readings in the Torah³⁴ not less than ten verses must be allotted.³⁵ Hezekiah³⁶ explained [the reason:³⁷ The number] corresponds to the Ten Commandments. But have we not learned in a Mishnah:³⁸ ‘On Sunday [the men of the *ma‘amad* read] *In the beginning*³⁹ and *Let there be a firmament*’⁴⁰ 5-8. although they contain not more than eight verses?⁴¹ R. Iddi said: Kahana and Ashë differ [on the method of procedure],⁴² one holding that [the reader] goes back, and the other maintaining that he cuts.⁴³ According to him who holds that ‘[the reader] goes back’, he repeats two verses;⁴⁴ and according to him who maintains that ‘he cuts’, *And there was evening and there was morning*⁴⁵ is a verse on its own.⁴⁶ But have we not learned in [the same] Mishnah: ‘On Monday [the reading of the *ma‘amad* was] *Let there be a firmament*⁴⁷ and *Let the waters … be gathered together’?*⁴⁸ 9-13, five verses. Now, according to him who holds that ‘[the reader] goes back’, he goes back over two verses, but according to him who maintains that ‘he cuts’, even if he does so, there are not ten⁴⁹ verses!⁵⁰, etc., forms the whole of verse 13 and cannot be reckoned as two verses as in the case of verses 5 and 8. R. Polippa b. R. Pariṭ raised an objection before R. Jonah,⁵¹ ‘Behold the section of Amalek!’⁵², which consists only of nine verses. ‘This is different’, he replied, ‘since⁵³ it is the prescribed portion for the day’.⁵⁴ R. Eliezer b. R. Maḳom raised an objection:⁵⁵ [It has been taught,] ‘He who reads the *hafṭarah* from the Prophet shall read not less than twenty-one verses’,⁵⁶, he must unroll it again, say the benedictions, and read that verse and the two verses which follow it’. This is a repetition of XI, 2, 6. but [if R. Huna’s ruling] is valid, twenty-three verses should be required, ten for the first three,⁵⁷ ten for the second three, and three for the last one!⁵⁸

³⁷ Why ten verses are required.
³⁸ Ta‘an. IV, 3 (Sonc. ed., p. 137).
³⁹ Gen. 1, 1-4.
⁴⁰ ibid.
⁴¹ How then could R. Huna rule that a minimum of ten verses was required?
⁴² As how to make ten verses out of eight.
⁴³ This is explained in the continuation.
⁴⁴ The second person repeats the last two of the three verses which the first had read. The two, together with the eight, make up the minimum of ten verses.
⁴⁵ The second part of each of verses 4 and 8 of Gen. 1.
⁴⁶ So that verses 4 and 8 represent two verses each, bringing the number to ten.
⁴⁷ Gen. 1, 6-8, three verses.
⁴⁸ ibid.
⁴⁹ Added by GRA, omitted by V, M and H.
⁵⁰ There would only be nine, since And there was evening
⁵¹ To R. Huna’s ruling.
⁵² Ex. 17, 8-16, read on Purim
⁵³ So GRA, M and H. V reads: ‘he said, No, it is taught, it’.
⁵⁴ This reply could also have been given to the preceding objection, since in Gen. 1, 6-13, if only verse 8 is divided into two, there would be nine verses, as in the section of Amalek.
⁵⁵ To R. Huna’s ruling.
⁵⁶ Meg. 23a (Sonc. ed., p. 140). From ‘but [if R. Huna’s ruling … to the last one]’ is added by GRA. V omits it but inserts in brackets: ‘It is permitted to skip in a Prophet but not in the Torah, because if [a reader] skipped even one verse by not reading it, and restored the scroll [to the ark] and recited ḳaddish
⁵⁷ In accordance with R. Huna’s ruling above that ‘for three readings … no less than ten verses must be allotted’.
⁵⁸ Since a minimum of three verses must be read for each person who is called up. No reply is given to the objection, but it is possible to provide the following explanation: Only when no more than three persons are called up is it necessary to allot ten verses, but when the number of persons is more than three, as, e.g., on the Sabbath when not less than seven persons must be called to the Torah, three verses for each person or twenty-one in all are sufficient.


###### Tractate Soferim 21:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:8)


מקום שנהגו לקרות את המגילה שני ימים קורין לילה ויום קורין הכל כמנהג המדינה וחייבין כל ישראל לקרותה בלשון הקודש:


Where the custom is to read the *Megillah* on two days⁵⁹. it should be so read, [and where it is customary to read it] in the night and in the day, it should be so read; all depends on the practice of the country. Furthermore, all Israel must read it in the holy tongue.⁶⁰

footnotes:
⁵⁹ The fourteenth and fifteenth of Adar
⁶⁰ In Hebrew.


###### Tractate Soferim 21:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Soferim/r/21:9)


האדם הגדול בענקים (הגדול היה אברהם אבינו גבוה יותר מענק) בענקים זה אברהם אבינו שהיה גבוה קומתו כנגד שבעים וארבעה אנשים ואכילתו ושתייתו כך היה כנגד שבעים וארבעה אנשים וכוחו כן מה עשה נטל שבעה עשר בני קטורה ובנה להן כרך של ברזל והכניסן לתוכה והשמש מעולם לא נכנס בתוכה מפני שהיא גבוה הרבה ומסר להן דיסקרין של אבנים טובות ומרגליות ומשתמשין לעתיד לבא כשעתיד הקב"ה להחפיר חמה ולבנה כמה דכתיב (ישעיהו כ״ד:כ״ג) וחפרה הלבנה ובושה החמה שמהן עתידין לשמש: עוג הוא אליעזר ופרסות רגליו אברהם אבינו היה טומנו בכף ידו ופעם אחת גער בו ומיראתו נפל ממנו שינו ונטלו אברהם אבינו ועשאו מטות שהן של שן והיה ישן שם ויש אומרים כסא עשאו וישב בו כל ימיו ומי נתנו לאברהם נמרוד והלך עוג ובנה ששים עיירות והקטן שבהן היה גובהן ששים מיל שנאמר (דברים ג׳:ד׳) ששים עיר כל חבל ארגוב ומה היה אכילתו אלף שורים וכן כל מין חיה ושתייתו אלף מידות ומה שהיתה טיפת זריעתו שלשים וששה ליטרין (וכן לכל דורות). תני מה עשה יעקב אבינו כשהביאו בניו את הכתונת בדם לא האמין להן כל עיקר מנין דכתיב (בראשית ל״ז:ל״ה) וימאן להתנחם לפי שאין מקבלין תנחומין על חי אבל שמת הוא מעצמו משתכח מן הלב שנאמר (תהילים ל״א:י״ג) נשכחתי כמת מלב ומה עשה הלך בהרים וחצב שנים עשר אבנים והעמידן שורה וכתב על כל אחת ואחת שם שבטו ושם מזלו ושם ירחו היאך (אלא) הוא על (האחת) האבן [האחת] ראובן טלה ניסן וכן לכל אבן ואבן התחיל משמעון ואמר להו גוזרני עליכם שתעמדו לראובן ולא עמדו לשמעון ולא עמדו לכל שבט ושבט ולא עמדו האבנים וכיון שהזכיר להם שם יוסף מיד עמדו וכרעו לפני אבן יוסף ועדיין לא ברור הדבר לו שהוא חי ועוד הלך ובחן באלומות כדעת הראשון וכתב עליהן שמות השבטים והמזלות והחדשים ואמר להן גוזרני עליכם שתשתחוו ללוי שהוא לבוש אורים ותומים [ולא] עמדו ליהודה שהוא מלך ולא עמדו וכשהזכיר להן יוסף עמדו וכרעו כולן ליוסף ועדיין לא נתברר כל עיקר שהוא חי אלא מן המשמרות אלישב כהן אלקנה שקנה פוטיפר (בראשית ל״ט:א׳) ויוסף הורד מצרימה ויקנהו פוטיפר וגו׳ (שם מא) (ויקרא פרעה שם יוסף צפנת פענח וגו׳). בת שלש שנים (ושלשה ימים) היתה רבקה כשיצאת מבית אביה שכן דרכן של מלכים כשתוליד להן בת מנהגן לשמוע לאחר ג׳ ימים ולפי שלא שמע אביה ולכך נעשה לה נס שלא תטמא שכך היה מנהגן של ארמיים לשכוב עם בנותיהם בתולות ואחר כך משיאין אותן (על שם דכתיב (שם כד) ואיש לא ידעה ואין איש אלא אביה). בת שש שנים היתה דינה כשהולידה את אסנת משכם מנין שש שנים של שכר הצאן שעבד יעקב אבינו ללבן והוסיף עשרים שנה שמת מכאן ואילך יוסיף המבין דעת וירד מיכאל והוליכה לבית פוטיפר:


⁶¹ *The greatest man among the Anakim*⁶²*—among the Anakim* refers to our father Abraham whose height was equal to that of seventy-four men; his eating and drinking were of a similar proportion, equal to those of seventy-four men; so too his strength. What did he do?⁶³. He removed the sixteen⁶⁴ sons of Keturah, built for them a walled city of iron and settled them in it. The sun never penetrated into it because it was exceedingly high, so Abraham handed to them disks of precious stones and pearls of which use will be made⁶⁵ in the hereafter when the Holy One, blessed be He, will cause the sun and moon to be confounded, as it is written, *When the moon shall be confounded, and the sun ashamed*,⁶⁶ because use will be made of these [disks as luminaries].
Og⁶⁷ is identical with Eliezer,⁶⁸ and [he was so huge that] he could hide Abraham’s feet in the palm of his hand. Once he was rebuked [by Abraham] and from fright his tooth fell out. Abraham picked it up and made ivory beds of it in which he slept. Others say that he made of it a chair which he used⁶⁹ all his life. Who gave him to Abraham? Nimrod.⁷⁰ Og went and built sixty cities, the smallest of which was sixty miles high, as it is stated, *Threescore cities, all the region of Argob*.⁷¹ And what did he eat? A thousand oxen and the same [number of] other animals, and his drink consisted of a thousand measures. A⁷² drop of his semen weighed thirty-six pounds.⁷³
It was taught: What did our father Jacob do when his sons brought him the coat [stained] with blood?⁷⁴ He did not believe them at all. Whence do we infer this? For it is written, *But he refused to be comforted*,⁷⁵ 35. because no consolations are acceptable for a living person. One, however, who is dead passes naturally from the mind,⁷⁶ as it is stated, *I am forgotten as a dead man out of mind*.⁷⁷ What then did he do? He proceeded to make a test with sheaves,⁷⁸ writing upon them the respective names of the tribes, their constellations and the months, and said to them, ‘I order you to prostrate yourselves before Levi because he wears the Urim and Thummim’,⁷⁹ but they did not stand up. ‘Before Judah who is king’, but they did not stand up; but when he mentioned Joseph to them, they all stood up and bowed before Joseph. But it was not yet quite clear that he was alive. So Jacob went to the mountains, hewed twelve stones, arranged them in a row, and wrote on each the name of its tribe, the name of its constellation and the name of its month. On one stone he wrote ‘Reuben, lamb, *Nisan*’⁸⁰ and similarly on every stone. He began from Simeon and said to them, ‘I order you to stand up for Reuben’, but they did not stand up. ‘For Simeon’, but they did not stand up. ‘For every tribe’, but the stones did not stand up. As soon, however, as he mentioned the name of Joseph to them, they stood up at once and bowed before Joseph’s stone.⁸¹ For this reason, all the tribes were written on Joseph’s stone. Similarly, all Israel are called by Joseph’s name, as it is stated, *Thou that leadest Joseph like a flock*.⁸² is a synonym of all Israel. So also all the heads of the families of the priests and Levites, [e.g.] *Eliashib*,⁸³ because of the phrase *lahashibo ’el ’abiw;*⁸⁴. The name Eliashib is broken up in three parts corresponding in sound and meaning to these three Heb. words. From ‘For this reason’ to *’abiw* is the reading of GRA. V and H have instead: ‘but from the *mishmaroth*, Eliashib the priest’. *Elḳanah*,⁸⁵, ‘he bought’. because Potiphar had bought him [as it is stated,] *And Joseph ms brought down to Egypt, and Potiphar … bought him*.⁸⁶, etc. (*ibid.* XLI, 45), which has no relevance to the subject.
When Rebekah left her father’s house⁸⁷ XXIV, 59ff. she was three years old,⁸⁸ because it is customary among kings, when a daughter is born to them, to hear of it after three days;⁸⁹ but as her father did not hear [of her birth] he did not defile her up to that time; and now a miracle happened to her in that her father died so that he should not defile her, as it is written, *Neither had any man known her*,⁹⁰ and by *man* only her father could be meant,⁹¹ for such was the practice of the Arameans to lie with their virgin daughters after they were three years of age,⁹² and then to give them away in marriage.⁹³, and by *man* only her father could be meant’.
Dinah was six years old when she bore Asenath from [her association with] Shechem,⁹⁴ corresponding to⁹⁵ the six years which Jacob served Laban in payment for the flock,⁹⁶ XXXI, 41. thus completing⁹⁷ the twenty years of his service. [The Archangel] Michael then descended and took her away to the house of Potiphar.
From here onward let the man of understanding increase knowledge.⁹⁸
